# Deterministic nonbipartite Ramanujan graphs in every fixed degree

{
  "abstract_verbatim": "For every fixed integer <i>d</i> ≥ 3, we give a deterministic algorithm that constructs a simple nonbipartite <i>d</i>-regular Ramanujan graph on every sufficiently large even number <i>n</i> of vertices. It outputs the full adjacency list in polynomially many bit operations, with an exponent that may depend on <i>d</i>. Every nonconstant adjacency eigenvalue lies strictly between $`-2\\sqrt{d-1}`$ and $`2\\sqrt{d-1}`$.",

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For every fixed integer <i>d</i> ≥ 3, we give a deterministic algorithm that constructs a simple nonbipartite <i>d</i>-regular Ramanujan graph on every sufficiently large even number <i>n</i> of vertices. It outputs the full adjacency list in polynomially many bit operations, with an exponent that may depend on <i>d</i>. Every nonconstant adjacency eigenvalue lies strictly between $`-2\sqrt{d-1}`$ and $`2\sqrt{d-1}`$.

- **claim-prescribed-even-order** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every integer $d\ge3$ there are constants $n_0(d)$ and $k_d$ and a deterministic algorithm with the following property. On input an even integer $n\ge n_0(d)$, the algorithm outputs the adjacency list of a simple $d$-regular graph $G$ on $n$ vertices in $O_d(n^{k_d})$ bit operations, and $$-2\sqrt{d-1}<\lambda<2\sqrt{d-1} \quad\text{for every eigenvalue $\lambda$ of }A_G\|_{\mathbf1^\perp}.$$ In particular, $G$ is connected and nonbipartite.
  - Explanation: Constructs the full adjacency list of a simple regular graph at every sufficiently large even order for a fixed degree. The strict bounds control both nonconstant spectral edges and imply connectedness and nonbipartiteness.
- **claim-target-size-rounding** (construction, [source](#stmt-cor-target-size)): For every fixed $d\ge3$, a deterministic polynomial-time algorithm, on every sufficiently large integer input $N$, produces a simple connected nonbipartite $d$-regular Ramanujan graph $G$ with $N\le \|V(G)\|\le N+1\le2N$.
  - Explanation: Rounds a sufficiently large requested size upward to the least even order and applies the main construction.
- **claim-discounted-selection** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-discounted-accounts)): At a state $x$, let $\Omega(x)$ be a nonempty finite set of candidate transitions with a specified probability distribution. For each index $i$ in a finite set let $Z_i(x)\ge1$ be a nonnegative account and let $D_i>0$ be its current discount. Fix nonnegative weights $a_i$. Set $$r_i=\max\left\{1,\frac{\mathbb E[Z_i(x')\mid x]}{Z_i(x)}\right\}, \qquad D_i'=D_i/r_i.$$ There is a candidate $x'$ such that $$\sum_i a_iD_i'Z_i(x')\le\sum_i a_iD_iZ_i(x).$$ In particular, selecting a minimizer of the left side has this property. If along the selected history $\mathbb E[Z_i(x')\mid x]\le(1+c_{i,t})Z_i(x)$ with $c_{i,t}\ge0$, then $$D_{i,t}^{-1}\le\prod_{j<t}(1+c_{i,j}) \le\exp\left(\sum_{j<t}c_{i,j}\right)$$ when the initial discount is one.
  - Explanation: Discounts each account by its own conditional expectation ratio. A minimizer of the common weighted total preserves the discounted budget, while separate growth estimates bound each discount along the selected history.
- **claim-scalar-upper-excess** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-scalar-excess)): Let an alive scalar quantity have current value $X$ and upper threshold $T$. On its ordinary next transitions suppose the threshold is unchanged and, for a fixed scale $b>0$, its increment $\Delta$ satisfies $$\|\Delta\|\le b,\qquad \mathbb E\Delta\le\mu, \qquad\mathbb E\Delta^2\le\nu, \qquad\mu,\nu\ge0.$$ For each fixed even integer $p\ge2$ there is $C_p$ such that the account $Z=1+((X-T)_+/b)^p$ obeys $$\mathbb EZ'\le \left[1+C_p\left(\frac\mu b+\frac\nu{b^2}\right)\right]Z.$$ The same upper bound holds if the ordinary estimates are conditional on an event, provided that on each other surviving transition the threshold is changed so that the account does not increase. A death transition may set the account to zero. For two-sided control apply the assertion to $X$ and $-X$ separately.
  - Explanation: Bounds the expectation growth of a positive-excess account from a bounded increment, an upper drift bound and a second-moment bound. Threshold adjustments handle exceptional surviving transitions, and death removes an account.
- **claim-prescribed-scale-excess** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-positive-excess-account)): Let $B_s>0$ and $\lambda>0$. Suppose an alive scalar quantity has an ordinary increment $\Delta$ with $\|\mathbb E\Delta\|\le\mu$, $\mathbb E\Delta^2\le\nu$, and $\|\Delta\|\le\lambda B_s$. For each fixed even $p\ge2$, the upper-excess account for either sign, measured in units $\lambda B_s$, has expectation factor at most $$1+C_p\left(\frac{\mu}{\lambda B_s} +\frac{\nu}{\lambda^2B_s^2}\right). \label{eq:original-34}$$ The threshold, exceptional-transition, and death conventions are those of Lemma <a href="#lem:scalar-excess" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:scalar-excess">4</a>.
  - Explanation: Specializes scalar excess control to the prescribed entry scale and applies it separately to both signs.
- **claim-configuration-exposure-identities** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lemma-configuration-algebra)): Let $C$ be the incidence matrix of all $dn$ stubs. On resolved stubs let $P_*$ exchange partners, and on $S$ let it be the averaging projection. Set $D_e=CP_*^2C^T=dI-\mathcal D$ and $$Q_0=I,\qquad Q_j=CP_*[(C^TC-I)P_*]^{j-1}C^T\quad(j\ge1).$$ Then, as a formal power series on $\mathbf 1^\perp$, $$W=\sum_{j\ge0}(\sigma h)^jQ_j.$$ For distinct $u,v\in S$, $l>2$, define $$U=[\xi_u,\xi_v],\quad G_0=I-J_2/l,\quad B=G_0^{-1}=I+J_2/(l-2),\quad H_0=-\sigma h FB,$$ where $J_2$ is the all-ones matrix and $F$ exchanges the two coordinates. If this pair is resolved, then $$\mathcal D'=\mathcal D-UBU^T,\qquad A_e'=A_e+UFBU^T,\qquad M'=M+U(hI-\sigma F)BU^T.$$ With $G=U^TWU$, $D_p=G-G_0$, and $H=(H_0^{-1}+D_p)^{-1}$, the inverse update, whenever its inverses exist, is $$W'=W-WUHU^TW.$$ Uniformly for the prescribed compact range of $h$, if $\\|D_p\\|=o(1)$, then $M'>0$ whenever $M>0$.
  - Explanation: Identifies the normalized inverse with a formal nonbacktracking path series and supplies exact exposure identities. A small pair-block deviation preserves the inertia needed for precision positivity.
- **claim-structural-accounts-and-separation** (construction, [source](#stmt-lemma-structure)): Fix $J=\lfloor .9\log_qn\rfloor$ and a sufficiently large fixed integer $r_*$. The joint discounted-account rule can maintain, through the early and cleanup phases, the following bound for every connected multigraph core $H$ of minimum degree at least two, excess $r=e_H-v_H+1\in\{1,\ldots,r_*\}$, and $s_H=e_H\le r_*J$: $$\#\{\text{stub embeddings of }H\} \le n^{1-r+o(1)}q^{s_H}. \label{eq:original-3}$$ An embedding is injective on vertices and uses distinct proper stubs for all incidences at a vertex. The accounts and their next-step averages are computable in polynomial time.* *At $l=l_1$, fewer than $b$ unresolved stubs are bad, where a stub is bad if its vertex has a nonempty actual nonbacktracking return of length at most $R_0$, or lies within distance $R_0$ of another unresolved stub (including distance zero). Cleanup can resolve all these bad stubs and stop at $l_0$ with one unresolved stub per unresolved vertex, and no nonempty actual nonbacktracking path of length at most $R_0$ joining two unresolved vertices, including a return to one vertex.
  - Explanation: Maintains computable core-embedding estimators through early pairing and cleanup. The proximity and return estimates leave enough cleanup steps to obtain distinct unresolved vertices without short joins or returns.
- **claim-absolute-path-majorants** (bound, [source](#stmt-lemma-path-majorants)): In the partial-configuration setting above, let an output label denote either a vertex coordinate projected onto $\mathbf1^\perp$, or a current centered stub $\xi_x$. For a label $i$ write $$p_i(x)=P_{i,\xi_x},\quad e_i(x)=E_{i,\xi_x},\quad w_i(x)=W_{i,\xi_x}.$$ We distinguish polynomial coefficient majorants from their numerical evaluations. Let $N_k(u,v)$ count actual nonbacktracking stub walks of length $k$ from $u$ to $v$, with $N_0(u,v)=\mathbf1_{u=v}$, and define the following polynomials with nonnegative coefficients: $$\begin{aligned} \mathsf U_l(z)&=\frac{C}{l} \left(1+\sum_{k=1}^{L-1}kq^kz^k\right),\\ \mathsf A_{i,x}(z)&=\sum_{k=0}^{L-1}N_k(v(i),v(x))z^k +\mathsf U_l(z),\\ \mathsf J_x(z)&=\sum_{k=1}^{L-1}N_k(v(x),v(x))z^k +\mathsf U_l(z),\qquad \mathsf B_{x,y}(z)=\mathsf A_{x,y}(z)+C/l. \end{aligned}$$ Here $v(i)$ is the underlying vertex of the output label. The constant $C$ is large enough to include the virtual, centering, and degree-zero Gram corrections. Coefficientwise absolute values of $p_i(x)$ are bounded by $\mathsf A_{i,x}$; diagonal and cross errors of $U^TPU-G_0$ are bounded by $\mathsf J_x$ and $\mathsf B_{x,y}$, respectively. The actual-walk counts are symmetric, as are these majorants whenever the input and output are stub labels. Write $$A_i(x)=\mathsf A_{i,x}(h),\qquad j_x=\mathsf J_x(h),\qquad b_{xy}=\mathsf B_{x,y}(h).$$ In particular $\mathsf U_l(h)\le C(1+L)^2q^{L/2}/l$, and $$\sum_{x\in S}A_i(x)\le C(1+L)^2q^{L/2}. \label{eq:path-row-sum}$$* *For the following bounds, assume that the current early or cleanup state has $l\ge l_0$, satisfies <a href="#eq:original-3" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:original-3">[eq:original-3]</a>, and has actual girth at least $g_0$. Uniformly through $l_0$, $$\begin{split} &\sum_{x\in S}A_i(x)^2\le C,\\ &\sum_{x\in S}j_x^2\le n^\eta,\qquad \sum_{x\in S}j_x\le n^\eta q^{L/2}. \end{split} \label{eq:original-4}$$ For an admissible pair in either phase there is a fixed $c_0'>0$ with $$j_u+j_v+b_{uv}\le n^{-c_0'}. \label{eq:original-5}$$ The same decay holds after multiplying nonconstant diagonal coefficients by any fixed power of their length, including for global diagonal entries with their degree-zero norm as baseline.
  - Explanation: Separates coefficientwise path majorants from their numerical evaluations. General row sums require no structural assumptions; stronger row-square, return and admissible-pair decay estimates require the stated core and girth bounds.
- **claim-pencil-coefficient-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-lemma-pencil-coefficients)): If the precision is positive for both signs at $h$, then $$\\|h^jQ_j\|_{\mathbf1^\perp}\\|\le C(1+j)^2, \qquad \\|P\\|\le C(1+L)^3. \label{eq:original-7}$$ For a fixed stub endpoint, the signed column of $P$ over global vertices has polylogarithmic squared norm. For the following absolute column and entry bounds, also assume that the current early or cleanup state has $l\ge l_0$, satisfies the core bound <a href="#eq:original-3" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:original-3">[eq:original-3]</a>, and has actual girth at least $g_0$. For every fixed integer $p\ge3$, the absolute $p$-power sum of that column is bounded by a constant; for sufficiently large $n$ that constant can depend only on $d$ and $p$. Stub columns have bounded absolute squared sums, and every outer entry of $P$ in the formal exposure expansion <a href="#eq:original-1" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:original-1">[eq:original-1]</a> has absolute value at most $1+o(1)$.
  - Explanation: Uses positivity of the signed pencil to control coefficient operators and the truncated path matrix. Structural assumptions supply the stronger absolute column and entry estimates.
- **claim-resolvent-bootstrap-consequences** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lemma-local-laws-implications)): At a current early or cleanup state, assume $l\ge l_0$, the core bound <a href="#eq:original-3" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:original-3">[eq:original-3]</a>, and actual girth at least $g_0$. Assume positivity and <a href="#eq:original-8" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:original-8">[eq:original-8]</a> at every tracked primary and auxiliary parameter, for both signs. For $B_h^+=\mathcal D+h^{-2}\alpha I$, the positive operator $S_h=(B_h^+)^{1/2}W(B_h^+)^{1/2}$ has norm at most $n^{v_*}/\alpha$. For a sufficiently large fixed $C$, the sum of its eigenvalues above $Cn^c/\alpha$ is at most $n^{v_*}/\alpha$. These bounds hold at the primaries and their auxiliaries. At all these points, every global coordinate or centered stub $f$ satisfies $$f^TW(\mathcal D+h^{-2}\alpha I)Wf \le(1+h^{-2})\\|f_\perp\\|^2+n^{-c_1}. \label{eq:original-10}$$ At the primaries, the averaged trace identity is $$\frac{\operatorname{Tr}K_{\rm all}}l+ h^{-2}\alpha\frac{\mathcal T}{l} =1+h^{-2}+O(n^{-c_2}). \label{eq:original-11}$$ The following moment bounds hold at both primaries and auxiliaries: $$\begin{split} \operatorname{Tr}K_{\rm all}^2+ \alpha^2\operatorname{Tr}Q_{\rm all}^2 &\le C\left(n^{2c}l/\alpha^2+n^{4v_*}/\alpha^4\right),\\ \operatorname{Tr}(\Gamma K_{\rm all})+ \alpha\operatorname{Tr}(\Gamma Q_{\rm all}) &\le C\left(n^cl/\alpha+n^{3v_*}/\alpha^3\right),\\ \\|Q_{\rm all}\\|&\le Cn^{2v_*}/\alpha^3. \end{split} \label{eq:original-12}$$ The decay exponents $c_1,c_2>0$ are fixed independently of $n$.
  - Explanation: Derives spectral norm and tail bounds, local quadratic estimates, an averaged trace identity and higher trace moments from current-state positivity and the bootstrap caps. The averaged identity is asserted at primary points.
- **claim-early-pairing** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-early-phase)): For the fixed parameters of the construction and all sufficiently large $n$, the simultaneous discounted-account rule can be run from the empty pairing down to $l_1$ unresolved stubs. It retains the structural estimates <a href="#eq:original-3" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:original-3">[eq:original-3]</a>–<a href="#eq:original-7" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:original-7">[eq:original-7]</a>, positivity of every tracked precision, and, at every tracked point, $$\|L_1\|\le n^{.075a}/\alpha_*,\qquad \|L_0\|\le n^{.105a}/\alpha_*^2,\qquad \max_{i,j}\|E_{ij}\|\le n^{.33a}/\sqrt{l_0}.$$ The low-square estimates <a href="#eq:original-23" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:original-23">[eq:original-23]</a> hold with any prescribed sufficiently small fixed loss exponent, for any fixed near radius needed in the proof. At every primary point, $$\mathcal T \ \ge\ n^{-\theta/2}(n^{-1}+\alpha/l)^{-1} \label{eq:original-13}$$ after a fixed constant is allowed in the right-hand side.
  - Explanation: Runs simultaneous deterministic pairing from the empty state to a sparse unresolved boundary while preserving structural estimates, signed positivity, traces, entry errors and low-square controls.
- **claim-uniform-pair-contractions** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-early-pair-contractions)): Let $A,C$ be centered $l$-by-$l$ matrices, with $A$ symmetric, and let a subscript $p$ denote their $\{u,v\}$ blocks. For a full uniform ordered distinct pair, $$\begin{split} \mathbb E\operatorname{Tr}(H_0A_p)&=0,\\ \mathbb E\operatorname{Tr}(BA_p)&=\frac{2\operatorname{Tr}A}{l-1},\\ \mathbb E\operatorname{Tr}(FA_pFC_p) &=\frac{2}{l(l-1)}\left( \operatorname{Tr}A\operatorname{Tr}C+ \operatorname{Tr}(AC)-2\sum_x A_{xx}C_{xx}\right). \end{split}$$ The last identity remains valid when $C$ is not symmetric. For a nonnegative summand, restriction to valid pairs or to survival costs a factor $1+O((D_g+1)/l)$. For a signed summand one must also subtract the omitted pairs before applying this factor.
  - Explanation: Computes exact ordered-distinct pair averages using matrix centering. Restricting signed sums requires subtracting omitted pairs before applying the density correction.
- **claim-early-trace-transitions** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-early-trace-transitions)): Under the current bootstrap bounds, the conditional means and second moments of the trace errors satisfy <a href="#eq:original-16" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:original-16">[eq:original-16]</a>–<a href="#eq:original-18" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:original-18">[eq:original-18]</a> at all tracked points. At primary points, $\mathcal T$ has the lower relative drift <a href="#eq:original-20" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:original-20">[eq:original-20]</a>, the second-moment bound <a href="#eq:early-T-variance" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:early-T-variance">[eq:early-T-variance]</a>, and relative step size $\lvert\Delta\mathcal T\rvert/\mathcal T=o(1)$. All error constants are uniform in the current state.
  - Explanation: Bounds conditional trace drifts and second moments under current bootstrap estimates, including the lower relative drift and small relative increments needed for reciprocal trace control.
- **claim-early-trace-accounts** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-early-trace-accounts)): The trace-square accounts and the inverse-$\mathcal T$ account have bounded discount loss under the bootstrap bounds and imply the trace conclusions of Proposition <a href="#prop:early-phase" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:early-phase">11</a> with a positive power of slack.
  - Explanation: Converts the trace transition estimates into bounded discount loss for trace-square and reciprocal accounts, leaving slack for extraction.
- **claim-early-index-word-classification** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-early-index-words)): Expand every occurrence of $H_0$ as $-\sigma h F-\sigma hJ_2/(l-2)$, every intermediate factor as $T+D_E$, and each outer factor as $P+E$. A resulting scalar word has two outer endpoint indices $s,t\in\{u,v\}$, alternating swap and intermediate factors, and one of the following descriptions.* 1. *It contains a non-pure swap $-\sigma hJ_2/(l-2)$.* 2. *All swaps are pure and it contains an off-diagonal $T$ factor, bounded by $b_{uv}$.* 3. *All swaps are pure, all $T$ factors are diagonal, and at least two intermediate factors are $D_E$.* 4. *All swaps are pure, all $T$ factors are diagonal, there is at least one intermediate factor, and there is at most one $D_E$ factor. If $s=t$, either there is a diagonal $T$ factor at $\bar s$, or the word has exactly one intermediate factor, namely $(D_E)_{\bar s\bar s}$. If $s\ne t$ and there is a $T$ factor, there is at least one local factor $j_u$ or $j_v$. If there is no $D_E$ factor, both local factors occur.* 5. *There are no intermediate factors. Its pure-swap portion has $s\ne t$.* *Here $\bar u=v$ and $\bar v=u$. The list is exhaustive; the cases are made disjoint by testing them in the displayed order. With only one intermediate $D_E$ and no $T$, the exceptional same-end word in item 4 is precisely $h^2 x_i(s)x_j(s)e_{\bar s}(\bar s)$, where $x_i,x_j$ are the chosen outer arrays, each a $p$ or $e$ row. All estimates obtained by retaining at most a fixed number of marked factors in these cases can be summed over the remaining words with a constant independent of $n$.
  - Explanation: Classifies every endpoint word in a fixed testing order. It isolates the same-end diagonal-error transfer and justifies summation of the unmarked factors by geometric convergence.
- **claim-early-signed-entry-mean** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-early-entry-mean)): For every existing output pair, conditionally on its survival, $$\big\|\mathbb E\Delta E_{ij}\big\|\le \mu_l, \qquad \mu_l=C\left[ \frac{n^{v_*}/\alpha_*+n^\eta q^{L/2}+D_g}{l^2} +\frac{n^\eta}{l^{3/2}}+\frac{d_m^2}{l}\right]. \label{eq:original-22}$$ This includes recentered stub outputs.
  - Explanation: Retains signed cancellations to bound the conditional mean of each surviving entry increment, including the recentering of stub outputs.
- **claim-short-path-residual** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-early-near-reference)): Fix an integer $k$. For $i\in X$, $u\in S$ at actual vertex distance at most $k$, remove from the actual part of $p_i(u)$ the monomial of the unique short path. The squared sum of the remaining coefficientwise absolute majorants over all these pairs is at most $$n^\eta+C(1+L)^4q^L/l.$$ Here $X=V$ or $S$, and $\eta$ can be any prescribed positive constant after the structural losses are reduced.
  - Explanation: Removes the unique short-path monomial before bounding the squared absolute residual over nearby outputs. This preserves the degree cancellation needed when new near pairs appear.
- **claim-early-low-square-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-early-low-squares)): For any fixed radius $R_c$, the simultaneous accounts retain $$\begin{split} &T_S=\sum_{x\in S}e_x(x)^2\le n^\eta, \qquad T_V=\sum_{i\in V}E_{ii}^2\le n^\eta n/l_0,\\ &T_{XS,k}=\sum_{\substack{i\in X,\ x\in S\\ \operatorname{dist}(\operatorname{vtx}i,\operatorname{vtx}x)\le k}} e_i(x)^2\le n^\eta, \qquad X\in\{V,S\},\quad 0\le k\le R_c . \end{split} \label{eq:original-23}$$ The final exponent $\eta>0$ may be prescribed in advance and made arbitrarily small.
  - Explanation: Controls surviving stub diagonals, global diagonals and nearby output-pair square sums through joint accounts with arbitrarily small fixed loss.
- **claim-interpolation-and-kernel-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-early-interpolation)): Let a measure of bounded mass satisfy $\int\|z\|^2\le n^{-\beta}$ and $\int\|z\|^p\le M$. For every fixed $0<t<p$, $$\int\|z\|^t\le n^{-\beta_t}(1+M)^{t/p}, \qquad \beta_t>0, \label{eq:original-28}$$ for sufficiently large $n$. If a nonnegative kernel $K$ on two probability spaces has both marginal densities at most $b$, then for nonnegative $f,g$ and $r,s\ge0$, $r+s\le1$, $$\iint K(x,y)f(x)^rg(y)^s\,dx\,dy \le b\left(\int f\right)^r\left(\int g\right)^s.$$ For $K(i,u)=\|p_i(u)\|^k$, $2\le k\le p$, with uniform $i\in X,u\in S$, one may use $b=n^{\eta_0}/l$ for any fixed $\eta_0>0$, once $n$ is sufficiently large. The row marginal alone is at most $C_p/l$. For $k=p$ or $2p$, the constants in the row and column sums can be bounded by a constant depending only on $d$, for each fixed $p$ and large $n$.
  - Explanation: Interpolates small second moments with high moments to obtain a power saving for intermediate powers. Marginal kernel bounds then control mixed output-input averages.
- **claim-early-entry-linearization** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-early-linearization)): For every surviving output pair, decompose its actual increment as $\Delta E_{ij}=B_e\mathcal L_{ij}+\mathcal N_{ij}$, where $\mathcal L$ is linear in $z$ and evaluated on the old output vectors. The residual contains the nonlinear terms, the formal tail, and the recentering of surviving stub outputs. It has sup $$\|\mathcal N_{ij}\|\le C(d_m^2+B_{\rm tail}+d_m/l)$$ and conditional second moment $$\mathbb E\|\mathcal N_{ij}\|^2 \le C(n^\eta/l^2+d_m^4/l). \label{eq:original-29}$$ The leading terms of $\mathcal L_{ij}$, up to their signs, are $$h p_i(u)z_{vj},\quad h p_i(v)z_{uj},\quad h p_j(u)z_{iv},\quad h p_j(v)z_{iu}, \label{eq:original-30}$$ and $$h^2p_i(s)p_j(t)z_{\bar s\bar t}, \qquad s,t\in\{u,v\}. \label{eq:original-31}$$ All other coefficients have sup bounded by a fixed negative power of $n$. A diagonal coefficient of $H_P$ satisfies, with its first endpoint $s$ fixed, $$\mathbb E_{\bar s}\bigl[\|(H_P)_{ss}\|^k \mathbf 1_{\rm valid}\bigr] \le C_p n^\eta n^{-c'_0(k-2)}/l, \qquad 2\le k\le p, \label{eq:original-32}$$ where the expectation may also omit boundedly many output labels.
  - Explanation: Separates the old-axis linear entry update from nonlinear errors, the formal truncation tail and recentering. The coefficient bounds identify which same-end terms gain a conditional moment saving.
- **claim-order-independent-high-moment-growth** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-early-high-moments)): There is a constant $C_*$, independent of every sufficiently large even order $p$, with the following property. After $p$ is fixed, one may choose a fixed $\kappa_p>0$, a fixed near radius $R_c$, and sufficiently small structural and low-square losses such that $$Z_p=1+F_p+\kappa_pD_p^{\rm mom},\qquad \mathbb E Z_p'\le(1+C_*/l+u_l)Z_p, \qquad \sum_lu_l=o(1), \label{eq:original-33}$$ with nonnegative deterministic upper budgets $u_l$ under the bootstrap assumptions.
  - Explanation: Weights pair and diagonal moment families so their transfers do not enlarge the common horizon-growth coefficient with the extraction order. The moment order is fixed before the near radius and loss parameters.
- **claim-cleanup-estimates** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-cleanup)): Assume the early-phase estimates at $l_1$, and maintain the structural estimators during cleanup. With the simultaneous discounted-account choice rule, the cleanup interval can be completed so that, at every chosen state, the precision matrices remain positive definite, <a href="#eq:original-8" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:original-8">[eq:original-8]</a> holds, and $$T_S=\sum_{x\in S}e_x(x)^2\leq n^\eta,\qquad \|E_{ij}\|\leq2B_k.$$ The second inequality holds for every surviving pair $(i,j)$ of total level $k=\nu(i)+\nu(j)$. At primary points the lower estimate for $\mathcal T$ persists in the form $$\mathcal T\geq n^{-\theta}(n^{-1}+\alpha/l)^{-1}.$$ In particular the consequences <a href="#eq:original-10" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:original-10">[eq:original-10]</a> and <a href="#eq:original-11" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:original-11">[eq:original-11]</a>, and a polynomial-loss form of <a href="#eq:original-13" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:original-13">[eq:original-13]</a>, hold at $l_0$.
  - Explanation: Completes cleanup while preserving positivity, bootstrap caps, diagonal squares and entry bounds indexed by label level. The primary squared-resolvent trace retains its lower bound.
- **claim-cleanup-pathwise-sums** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cleanup-pathwise)): Fix any chosen cleanup history up to its current state, and fix a label $i$ while it is alive. With $u_t$ the prescribed first endpoint of step $t$, one has $$\sum_t A_i^t(u_t)^2\leq n^\eta,\qquad \sum_t (j_{u_t}^t)^2\leq n^\eta. \label{eq:original-36}$$ The first estimate also holds with $A_i^t(u_t)$ replaced by $r_i^t(u_t)$. For every fixed initial stub $v$, whether or not it has already been resolved, the actual-path part of $b_{u_t v}^t$ satisfies the same squared-sum estimate over $t$.
  - Explanation: Bounds majorants along distinct prescribed first endpoints by paths present in the graph at the current history endpoint. The bound uses no future completion.
- **claim-cleanup-scalar-persistence** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cleanup-traces)): At a current cleanup state, assume positive definiteness and <a href="#eq:original-8" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:original-8">[eq:original-8]</a> at every tracked point for both signs, together with the level-dependent entry and $T_S$ bootstrap bounds. The trace-square accounts at all tracked points and the $\mathcal T$-deviation accounts at the primaries have bounded discount loss at scales strictly inside the corresponding bounds of Proposition <a href="#prop:cleanup" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:cleanup">22</a>.
  - Explanation: Preserves trace-error accounts at tracked points and squared-resolvent deviation accounts at primaries despite the prescribed first endpoint.
- **claim-cleanup-endpoint-word-classes** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-cleanup-words)): After expansion of a numerical update difference and of its formal truncation tail, every term is covered by the following classes:* 1. *a term containing a non-pure swap $R$, and hence a factor $C/l$;* 2. *a pure-swap term with no intermediate endpoint factor;* 3. *a pure-swap term with exactly one endpoint $E$ factor and no topology factor;* 4. *a term with a cross topology factor $b_{uv}$;* 5. *a pure-swap term with local topology factors only, and at most one endpoint $E$ factor;* 6. *a term with at least two endpoint $E$ factors.* *In class 3 the single endpoint $E$ factor may be grouped with $W$ outers. For class 5, if both outers are at $u$, an opposite local factor $j_v$ is necessary; the symmetric statement holds for two outers at $v$. If class 5 has no endpoint $E$ factor and has distinct outers, local factors at both endpoints are necessary. All remaining factors and choices of their positions can be summed with a bounded geometric multiplier.
  - Explanation: Groups numerical update differences and formal tails by endpoint error and topology factors. Pure-swap parity forces an opposite local factor in the same-end topology cases.
- **claim-cleanup-diagonal-budgets** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cleanup-diagonal)): Under the cleanup bootstrap, through every chosen history segment, $$\sum_t\\|m\\|_2\leq n^\eta,\qquad \sum_tV_t\leq n^\eta+Cf_bT_{\mathrm{cap}},$$ where $T_{\mathrm{cap}}$ is the chosen bootstrap cap for $T_S$.
  - Explanation: Bounds cumulative signed-mean norms and second moments for surviving stub diagonals. The resulting aggregate account closes the diagonal-square bootstrap.
- **claim-cleanup-level-sensitive-jumps** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cleanup-jumps)): For a surviving entry $E_{ij}$ of total level $k$, every allowed cleanup step satisfies $$\|\Delta E_{ij}\|/B_k\leq n^{-2\rho}$$ for all sufficiently large $n$.
  - Explanation: Shows that each normalized surviving entry jump is power-small. Transfers to lower-level labels are paid by the gap between their scales; nondecreasing transfers have small coefficients.
- **claim-cleanup-predictable-entry-budgets** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cleanup-entry-budgets)): For every fixed entry while its labels are alive, and through every chosen history segment, there is a fixed $c_5>0$ such that $$\begin{split} &\sum_{\substack{t:\text{nonspecial survival}\\ \text{has positive probability}}} \frac{\bigl\|\mathbb E(\Delta E_{ij}\mid \text{nonspecial survival})\bigr\|}{B_k} \leq n^{-c_5},\\ &\sum_{\substack{t:\text{nonspecial survival}\\ \text{has positive probability}}} \frac{\mathbb E((\Delta E_{ij})^2\mid \text{nonspecial survival})}{B_k^2} \leq n^{-c_5}. \end{split} \label{eq:original-39}$$ Here $k=\nu(i)+\nu(j)$ is the pair’s total level. Each sum stops when one of its labels dies.
  - Explanation: Bounds cumulative normalized drift and second moments for ordinary surviving entry updates. The sums include every step where nonspecial survival has positive conditional probability and stop at label death.
- **claim-separated-boundary-completion** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-boundary-completion)): Under these inputs, the account rule can deterministically complete the pairing. Every added edge is simple and preserves the girth exclusion. At the end the precision is positive at every primary point for both signs. There is a reference matrix $\widehat P_{\rm fin}$, defined below by exact numerical updates from a smooth coefficient truncation at the stop, such that at every retained primary parameter and both signs, $$\max_{i,j}\|(W_{\rm fin}-\widehat P_{\rm fin})_{ij}\| \le C B_B, \qquad B_B=\frac{n^{.40a}}{\sqrt{l_2}}.$$ The construction retains the stop configuration and the matching added after it. These define the same family $\widehat P_{\rm fin}(h)$ throughout the primary interval. All the accounts, candidate values and transitions in this section can be evaluated in a number of bit operations polynomial in $n$, with an exponent depending only on the fixed parameters.
  - Explanation: Completes the separated boundary with simple edges and signed positive precisions. It retains a single smoothly parameterized reference determined by the stop configuration and the completed matching.
- **claim-smooth-reference-sparse-rows** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-boundary-smooth-reference)): There are constants $r_P'>0$ and $C$ such that $$\widehat\Gamma_0:=V_0^T\widehat P_0V_0 =I+\widehat D_0\quad\hbox{on }\mathcal H_0, \qquad \\|\widehat D_0\\|\le \frac{C}{R_0}. \label{eq:original-40}$$ For every global vertex coordinate $i$, the vector $p_{i,0}=V_0^T\widehat P_0e_i$ satisfies $$p_{i,0}=c_i\Pi_{l_0}e_{s(i)}+\tau_i, \qquad \|c_i\|\le1,\qquad \\|\tau_i\\|_2\le n^{-r_P'}. \label{eq:original-41}$$ The first summand is omitted if there is no actual path from $i$ to a boundary vertex of length less than $\lfloor R_0/4\rfloor$. Otherwise that vertex and that path are unique, and $c_i=(\sigma h)^{\operatorname{dist}(i,s(i))}$. For a boundary vertex, $s(i)$ is its own stub and $c_i=1$. The estimate for $\tau_i$ holds also for $\partial_h\tau_i$, after decreasing $r_P'$ if necessary. All these statements hold uniformly throughout the closed convex hull of the retained primary parameters.
  - Explanation: Controls the compression of the smooth stop reference in operator norm and decomposes every global boundary row into a unique short coordinate and a power-small tail. The tail derivative obeys the same type of estimate.
- **claim-boundary-pair-averages** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-boundary-pair-averages)): Suppose that $D\mathbf1=0$, $\max_x(D^2)_{xx}\le C_0$ and $\max_{x,y}\|D_{xy}\|\le\epsilon=o(1)$. Then $\\|H\\|\le C$, and $$\|H_{uu}\|\le C(l^{-1}+\|D_{vv}\|+\|D_{uv}\|).$$ The conditional absolute mean of $(H-H_0)_{uu}$ with $u$ fixed is at most $C(1+\sqrt T)/\sqrt l$, and the conditional second moment of $H_{uu}$ is at most $C(1+T)/l$. The kernel $\|(H-H_0)_{uv}\|$ has every row and column sum at most $C(1+\sqrt T)\sqrt l$. After subtracting the single-$A$ term $-H_0AH_0$, these absolute bounds gain a factor $C\epsilon$. All these statements remain valid if the possible partner set omits a fixed number of coordinates.* *If $x,y$ are arbitrary scalar arrays and $\mathcal I$ is the allowed endpoint set, $m=\|\mathcal I\|$, then the pure-swap single-$A$ contraction has the exact formula $$\mathbb E_{\mathcal I} [(I^Tx)^TF A F(I^Ty)] =\frac{2}{m(m-1)}\left[ Y_{\mathcal I}\langle x_{\mathcal I},y_{\mathcal I}\rangle +x_{\mathcal I}^TD_{\mathcal I\mathcal I}y_{\mathcal I} -2\sum_{u\in\mathcal I}D_{uu}x_uy_u\right],$$ where $Y_{\mathcal I}=\sum_{u\in\mathcal I}D_{uu}$. For full pairs the following additional identities hold: $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb E\operatorname{Tr}(F A F K) &=\frac{2}{l(l-1)} \left[YX+\operatorname{Tr}D^3 -2\sum_uD_{uu}(D^2)_{uu}\right],\\ \mathbb E\operatorname{Tr}(F K F K) &=\frac{2}{l(l-1)} \left[X^2+\operatorname{Tr}D^4 -2\sum_u(D^2)_{uu}^2\right],\\ \mathbb E[(I^Tr)^TF K F(I^Tr)] &=\frac{2}{l(l-1)} \left[gX+r^TD^2r-2\sum_ur_u^2(D^2)_{uu}\right], \qquad g=\\|r\\|^2. \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Combines small-entry Neumann expansions with exact ordered-distinct contractions. It controls diagonal coefficients, remainder kernels and signed trace averages without treating matrix entries as independent.
- **claim-local-projector-from-frozen-reference** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-repair-projector)): Assume $x_i^\sigma>-e/20$ for all nonconstant indices $i$ and both signs. Suppose the final resolvents $R_\sigma(z)=(z-\sigma A_G)^{-1}\|_{\mathbf 1^\perp}$ at the primary points $z=z(h)$ satisfy the entry comparison $$\max_p\left\| R_\sigma(z(h))_{pp}-m_h\widehat P_{\mathrm{fin},pp}^\sigma(h) \right\|\le C B_B, \qquad B_B=o(\sqrt e).$$ Suppose also that the same frozen reference is defined throughout the primary range and obeys $$\sup_{h,p}\left\| \frac{d}{dh}\bigl(m_h\widehat P_{\mathrm{fin},pp}^\sigma(h)\bigr) \right\|\le C.$$ Assume the primary grid has bounded geometric spacing, as in the construction. Then, uniformly in $p$ and $\sigma$, $$\sum_{i:\,x_i^\sigma\le w}\|v_i(p)\|^2\le Cw^{3/2} \qquad (2e\le w\le c_d). \label{eq:original-61}$$ The constant can be enlarged to make the same assertion valid for all larger $w$ in the bounded spectral range.
  - Explanation: Uses positive resolvent differences at geometrically separated primary gaps and the derivative of the same frozen reference to obtain local spectral mass bounds near either edge.
- **claim-bulk-resolvent-and-typical-edges** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-repair-bulk)): The projector estimate <a href="#eq:original-61" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:original-61">[eq:original-61]</a> implies $$\sup_p\bigl[(R_B^\sigma)^2\bigr]_{pp} \le \frac{C}{\sqrt{W_b}}. \label{eq:original-63}$$ If $w_p\asymp W_b$ is a primary gap, every entry of $R_B^\sigma-R_\sigma(s+w_p)$ is $O(\sqrt{W_b})$.* *For the typical-edge assertion below, the additional inputs are the following specific conclusions at the separation stop:* 1. *Only $O(l_0)$ edges of $G$ were added after the stop, with $l_0\asymp n^{2/3+.15a}$. At most $n^{.08+o(1)}$ stop vertices have a nonempty actual nonbacktracking return of length at most $r_n=\lfloor .08\log_q n\rfloor+2$.* 2. *The number of actual nonbacktracking paths of any length $k<L$ between fixed endpoints is bounded by a constant. The stop reference is the weighted sum of these paths with coefficient $\varphi(k/L)(\sigma h)^k$, up to a matrix whose entries are $o(n^{-.04})$. The taper is bounded and equals one for $k\le r_n$, and $h\le h_c$.* 3. *The final reference has the form $\widehat P_{\mathrm{fin}}=\widehat P_0- \widehat P_0V_0H_fV_0^T\widehat P_0$, where $V_0$ is an isometry from an $(l_0-1)$-dimensional space, $\\|H_f\\|\le C$, and $\\|\widehat P_0\\|\le C(1+L)^3$. At the comparison primary points the final resolvent has entry error $O(B_B)=o(n^{-.04})$ relative to $m_h\widehat P_{\mathrm{fin}}$.* *The return count, multiplicity and virtual-path assertions are consequences of Lemmas <a href="#lemma:structure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lemma:structure">7</a> and <a href="#lemma:path-majorants" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lemma:path-majorants">8</a>; the norm bound is Lemma <a href="#lemma:pencil-coefficients" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lemma:pencil-coefficients">9</a>; the reference representation and final error are furnished by Section <a href="#sec:boundary-completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:boundary-completion">7</a> and Proposition <a href="#prop:boundary-completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:boundary-completion">29</a>. All inputs are uniform in the primary points used below. For each pair of cutoffs as above, all but a fraction $O(n^{-.1})$ of the oriented edges $(p,p')$ have, for both signs, their endpoint block of $R_B^\sigma$ within $n^{-.024}$ of $P_{\mathrm{tree}}^\sigma$. The estimates are uniform in the cutoffs; their exceptional sets may depend on the cutoffs.
  - Explanation: The projector estimate bounds squared bulk-resolvent rows and their comparison with a nearby full resolvent. The stronger typical-edge conclusion additionally uses separation-stop paths and the frozen-reference representation; exceptional sets may depend on the cutoffs.
- **claim-exact-simultaneous-switch-repair** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-thm-exact-repair)): Fix an integer $d\ge3$ and a constant $C_0\ge1$. Put $$\begin{gathered} q=d-1,\qquad s=2\sqrt q,\qquad a=10^{-4},\\ e=n^{-2/3+a},\qquad W_b=n^{-2/3+100a},\qquad M=\lceil n^{1/3+10a}\rceil. \end{gathered}$$ There are constants $n_0,k$, depending only on $d,C_0$, and a deterministic algorithm with the following property for $n\ge n_0$.* *Let $G$ be a simple $d$-regular graph on $n$ vertices. Let $v_2,\ldots,v_n$ be an orthonormal eigenbasis of $A_G$ on $\mathbf1^\perp$, with eigenvalues $\lambda_i$, and put $x_i^\sigma=s-\sigma\lambda_i$ for $\sigma\in\{+1,-1\}$. Assume the following three properties.* 1. **Spectral confinement.* For every $i=2,\ldots,n$ and both signs, $x_i^\sigma>-e/20$.* 2. **Local spectral mass.* For every vertex $p$, both signs, and every $w\in[2e,2d]$, $$\sum_{\substack{2\le i\le n\\x_i^\sigma\le w}}\|v_i(p)\|^2 \le C_0w^{3/2}.$$* 3. **Typical edge blocks.* For every pair $w_B^\sigma\in[W_b,2W_b]\setminus\{x_i^\sigma:2\le i\le n\}$, define $$R_B^\sigma=\sum_{\substack{2\le i\le n\\x_i^\sigma\ge w_B^\sigma}} \frac{v_iv_i^T}{x_i^\sigma}.$$ All but a fraction $C_0n^{-.1}$ of the $dn$ oriented edges $(p,p')$ satisfy the following bound for both signs: $$\left\\| \begin{pmatrix}(R_B^\sigma)_{pp}&(R_B^\sigma)_{pp'}\\ (R_B^\sigma)_{p'p}&(R_B^\sigma)_{p'p'}\end{pmatrix} -\frac1{d-2}\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt q&\sigma\\\sigma&\sqrt q\end{pmatrix} \right\\|_{\max}\le n^{-.024}.$$ Here $\\|\cdot\\|_{\max}$ is the largest absolute entry. The exceptional edge set may depend on the chosen pair of cutoffs.* *Given the adjacency list of $G$, the algorithm finds $M$ vertex-disjoint switches, each deleting two edges and inserting two edges. In $O_{d,C_0}(n^k)$ bit operations it outputs the adjacency list of a simple $d$-regular graph $G'$ on the same vertices such that $$-2\sqrt{d-1}<\lambda_i(G')<2\sqrt{d-1} \qquad (2\le i\le n).$$ In particular, $G'$ is connected and nonbipartite.
  - Explanation: Repairs a simple regular graph satisfying confinement, local spectral mass and typical bulk-block assumptions. A biased edge-pair law supplies a positive mean correction at both edges, and finite deterministic enumeration finds vertex-disjoint valid switches passing an exact spectral test.
- **claim-polynomial-bit-implementation** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-bit-complexity)): For each fixed $d$ and the fixed choices in Section <a href="#subsec:hierarchy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="subsec:hierarchy">2.3</a>, every stage of the preceding construction is implementable with polynomially many bit operations in $n$.
  - Explanation: Implements every stage with rational parameters, finite graph operations, fixed moments, exact account comparisons and rational matrix computations. The degree and analytic choices remain fixed throughout the bit-complexity analysis.

On its ordinary next transitions suppose the threshold is unchanged and, for a fixed scale $b>0$, its increment $\\Delta$ satisfies $$|\\Delta|\\le b,\\qquad \\mathbb E\\Delta\\le\\mu,\n \\qquad\\mathbb E\\Delta^2\\le\\nu,\n \\qquad\\mu,\\nu\\ge0.$$ For each fixed even integer $p\\ge2$ there is $C_p$ such that the account $Z=1+((X-T)_+/b)^p$ obeys $$\\mathbb EZ'\\le\n \\left[1+C_p\\left(\\frac\\mu b+\\frac\\nu{b^2}\\right)\\right]Z.$$ The same upper bound holds if the ordinary estimates are conditional on an event, provided that on each other surviving transition the threshold is changed so that the account does not increase. A death transition may set the account to zero. For two-sided control apply the assertion to $X$ and $-X$ separately.",
      "explanation": "Bounds the expectation growth of a positive-excess account from a bounded increment, an upper drift bound and a second-moment bound. Threshold adjustments handle exceptional surviving transitions, and death removes an account.",

      "hypotheses": [

        }

      "id": "claim-scalar-upper-excess",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-positive-excess-account",
        "exact_quote": "Let $B_s>0$ and $\\lambda>0$. Suppose an alive scalar quantity has an ordinary increment $\\Delta$ with $|\\mathbb E\\Delta|\\le\\mu$, $\\mathbb E\\Delta^2\\le\\nu$, and $|\\Delta|\\le\\lambda B_s$. For each fixed even $p\\ge2$, the upper-excess account for either sign, measured in units $\\lambda B_s$, has expectation factor at most $$1+C_p\\left(\\frac{\\mu}{\\lambda B_s}\n             +\\frac{\\nu}{\\lambda^2B_s^2}\\right).\n \\label{eq:original-34}$$ The threshold, exceptional-transition, and death conventions are those of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:scalar-excess\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:scalar-excess\">4</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-178.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-positive-excess-account",
          "exact_quote": "Let $B_s>0$ and $\\lambda>0$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lemma-configuration-algebra",
        "exact_quote": "Let $C$ be the incidence matrix of all $dn$ stubs. On resolved stubs let $P_*$ exchange partners, and on $S$ let it be the averaging projection. Set $D_e=CP_*^2C^T=dI-\\mathcal D$ and $$Q_0=I,\\qquad\n Q_j=CP_*[(C^TC-I)P_*]^{j-1}C^T\\quad(j\\ge1).$$ Then, as a formal power series on $\\mathbf 1^\\perp$, $$W=\\sum_{j\\ge0}(\\sigma h)^jQ_j.$$ For distinct $u,v\\in S$, $l>2$, define $$U=[\\xi_u,\\xi_v],\\quad\n G_0=I-J_2/l,\\quad B=G_0^{-1}=I+J_2/(l-2),\\quad\n H_0=-\\sigma h FB,$$ where $J_2$ is the all-ones matrix and $F$ exchanges the two coordinates. If this pair is resolved, then $$\\mathcal D'=\\mathcal D-UBU^T,\\qquad\n A_e'=A_e+UFBU^T,\\qquad\n M'=M+U(hI-\\sigma F)BU^T.$$ With $G=U^TWU$, $D_p=G-G_0$, and $H=(H_0^{-1}+D_p)^{-1}$, the inverse update, whenever its inverses exist, is $$W'=W-WUHU^TW.$$ Uniformly for the prescribed compact range of $h$, if $\\|D_p\\|=o(1)$, then $M'>0$ whenever $M>0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-178.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

If this pair is resolved, then $$\\mathcal D'=\\mathcal D-UBU^T,\\qquad\n A_e'=A_e+UFBU^T,\\qquad\n M'=M+U(hI-\\sigma F)BU^T.$$ With $G=U^TWU$, $D_p=G-G_0$, and $H=(H_0^{-1}+D_p)^{-1}$, the inverse update, whenever its inverses exist, is $$W'=W-WUHU^TW.$$ Uniformly for the prescribed compact range of $h$, if $\\|D_p\\|=o(1)$, then $M'>0$ whenever $M>0$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lemma-structure",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $J=\\lfloor .9\\log_qn\\rfloor$ and a sufficiently large fixed integer $r_*$. The joint discounted-account rule can maintain, through the early and cleanup phases, the following bound for every connected multigraph core $H$ of minimum degree at least two, excess $r=e_H-v_H+1\\in\\{1,\\ldots,r_*\\}$, and $s_H=e_H\\le r_*J$: $$\\#\\{\\text{stub embeddings of }H\\}\n       \\le n^{1-r+o(1)}q^{s_H}.\n \\label{eq:original-3}$$ An embedding is injective on vertices and uses distinct proper stubs for all incidences at a vertex. The accounts and their next-step averages are computable in polynomial time.*\n\n*At $l=l_1$, fewer than $b$ unresolved stubs are bad, where a stub is bad if its vertex has a nonempty actual nonbacktracking return of length at most $R_0$, or lies within distance $R_0$ of another unresolved stub (including distance zero). Cleanup can resolve all these bad stubs and stop at $l_0$ with one unresolved stub per unresolved vertex, and no nonempty actual nonbacktracking path of length at most $R_0$ joining two unresolved vertices, including a return to one vertex.",
        "source_id": "PM-178.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lemma-structure",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $J=\\lfloor .9\\log_qn\\rfloor$ and a sufficiently large fixed integer $r_*$. The accounts and their next-step averages are computable in polynomial time.*\n\n*At $l=l_1$, fewer than $b$ unresolved stubs are bad, where a stub is bad if its vertex has a nonempty actual nonbacktracking return of length at most $R_0$, or lies within distance $R_0$ of another unresolved stub (including distance zero). Cleanup can resolve all these bad stubs and stop at $l_0$ with one unresolved stub per unresolved vertex, and no nonempty actual nonbacktracking path of length at most $R_0$ joining two unresolved vertices, including a return to one vertex.",
          "source_id": "PM-178.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix $J=\\lfloor .9\\log_qn\\rfloor$ and a sufficiently large fixed integer $r_*$. The accounts and their next-step averages are computable in polynomial time.*\n\n*At $l=l_1$, fewer than $b$ unresolved stubs are bad, where a stub is bad if its vertex has a nonempty actual nonbacktracking return of length at most $R_0$, or lies within distance $R_0$ of another unresolved stub (including distance zero). Cleanup can resolve all these bad stubs and stop at $l_0$ with one unresolved stub per unresolved vertex, and no nonempty actual nonbacktracking path of length at most $R_0$ joining two unresolved vertices, including a return to one vertex.",
      "explanation": "Maintains computable core-embedding estimators through early pairing and cleanup. The proximity and return estimates leave enough cleanup steps to obtain distinct unresolved vertices without short joins or returns.",

      "id": "claim-structural-accounts-and-separation",
      "kind": "construction",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lemma-structure",
          "exact_quote": "for every connected multigraph core $H$ of minimum degree at least two, excess $r=e_H-v_H+1\\in\\{1,\\ldots,r_*\\}$, and $s_H=e_H\\le r_*J$:",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lemma-path-majorants",
        "exact_quote": "In the partial-configuration setting above, let an output label denote either a vertex coordinate projected onto $\\mathbf1^\\perp$, or a current centered stub $\\xi_x$. For a label $i$ write $$p_i(x)=P_{i,\\xi_x},\\quad e_i(x)=E_{i,\\xi_x},\\quad\n w_i(x)=W_{i,\\xi_x}.$$ We distinguish polynomial coefficient majorants from their numerical evaluations. Let $N_k(u,v)$ count actual nonbacktracking stub walks of length $k$ from $u$ to $v$, with $N_0(u,v)=\\mathbf1_{u=v}$, and define the following polynomials with nonnegative coefficients: $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathsf U_l(z)&=\\frac{C}{l}\n       \\left(1+\\sum_{k=1}^{L-1}kq^kz^k\\right),\\\\\n \\mathsf A_{i,x}(z)&=\\sum_{k=0}^{L-1}N_k(v(i),v(x))z^k\n                         +\\mathsf U_l(z),\\\\\n \\mathsf J_x(z)&=\\sum_{k=1}^{L-1}N_k(v(x),v(x))z^k\n                         +\\mathsf U_l(z),\\qquad\n \\mathsf B_{x,y}(z)=\\mathsf A_{x,y}(z)+C/l.\n \\end{aligned}$$ Here $v(i)$ is the underlying vertex of the output label. The constant $C$ is large enough to include the virtual, centering, and degree-zero Gram corrections. Coefficientwise absolute values of $p_i(x)$ are bounded by $\\mathsf A_{i,x}$; diagonal and cross errors of $U^TPU-G_0$ are bounded by $\\mathsf J_x$ and $\\mathsf B_{x,y}$, respectively. The actual-walk counts are symmetric, as are these majorants whenever the input and output are stub labels. Write $$A_i(x)=\\mathsf A_{i,x}(h),\\qquad\n j_x=\\mathsf J_x(h),\\qquad b_{xy}=\\mathsf B_{x,y}(h).$$ In particular $\\mathsf U_l(h)\\le C(1+L)^2q^{L/2}/l$, and $$\\sum_{x\\in S}A_i(x)\\le C(1+L)^2q^{L/2}.\n \\label{eq:path-row-sum}$$*\n\n\n\n*For the following bounds, assume that the current early or cleanup state has $l\\ge l_0$, satisfies <a href=\"#eq:original-3\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:original-3\">[eq:original-3]</a>, and has actual girth at least $g_0$. Uniformly through $l_0$, $$\\begin{split}\n &\\sum_{x\\in S}A_i(x)^2\\le C,\\\\\n &\\sum_{x\\in S}j_x^2\\le n^\\eta,\\qquad\n   \\sum_{x\\in S}j_x\\le n^\\eta q^{L/2}.\n \\end{split}\n \\label{eq:original-4}$$ For an admissible pair in either phase there is a fixed $c_0'>0$ with $$j_u+j_v+b_{uv}\\le n^{-c_0'}.\n \\label{eq:original-5}$$ The same decay holds after multiplying nonconstant diagonal coefficients by any fixed power of their length, including for global diagonal entries with their degree-zero norm as baseline.",
        "source_id": "PM-178.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

Let $N_k(u,v)$ count actual nonbacktracking stub walks of length $k$ from $u$ to $v$, with $N_0(u,v)=\\mathbf1_{u=v}$, and define the following polynomials with nonnegative coefficients: $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathsf U_l(z)&=\\frac{C}{l}\n       \\left(1+\\sum_{k=1}^{L-1}kq^kz^k\\right),\\\\\n \\mathsf A_{i,x}(z)&=\\sum_{k=0}^{L-1}N_k(v(i),v(x))z^k\n                         +\\mathsf U_l(z),\\\\\n \\mathsf J_x(z)&=\\sum_{k=1}^{L-1}N_k(v(x),v(x))z^k\n                         +\\mathsf U_l(z),\\qquad\n \\mathsf B_{x,y}(z)=\\mathsf A_{x,y}(z)+C/l.\n \\end{aligned}$$ Here $v(i)$ is the underlying vertex of the output label. The constant $C$ is large enough to include the virtual, centering, and degree-zero Gram corrections. Coefficientwise absolute values of $p_i(x)$ are bounded by $\\mathsf A_{i,x}$; diagonal and cross errors of $U^TPU-G_0$ are bounded by $\\mathsf J_x$ and $\\mathsf B_{x,y}$, respectively. The actual-walk counts are symmetric, as are these majorants whenever the input and output are stub labels. Write $$A_i(x)=\\mathsf A_{i,x}(h),\\qquad\n j_x=\\mathsf J_x(h),\\qquad b_{xy}=\\mathsf B_{x,y}(h).$$ In particular $\\mathsf U_l(h)\\le C(1+L)^2q^{L/2}/l$, and $$\\sum_{x\\in S}A_i(x)\\le C(1+L)^2q^{L/2}.\n \\label{eq:path-row-sum}$$*\n\n\n\n*For the following bounds, assume that the current early or cleanup state has $l\\ge l_0$, satisfies <a href=\"#eq:original-3\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:original-3\">[eq:original-3]</a>, and has actual girth at least $g_0$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lemma-pencil-coefficients",
        "exact_quote": "If the precision is positive for both signs at $h$, then $$\\|h^jQ_j|_{\\mathbf1^\\perp}\\|\\le C(1+j)^2,\n \\qquad \\|P\\|\\le C(1+L)^3.\n \\label{eq:original-7}$$ For a fixed stub endpoint, the signed column of $P$ over global vertices has polylogarithmic squared norm. For the following absolute column and entry bounds, also assume that the current early or cleanup state has $l\\ge l_0$, satisfies the core bound <a href=\"#eq:original-3\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:original-3\">[eq:original-3]</a>, and has actual girth at least $g_0$. For every fixed integer $p\\ge3$, the absolute $p$-power sum of that column is bounded by a constant; for sufficiently large $n$ that constant can depend only on $d$ and $p$. Stub columns have bounded absolute squared sums, and every outer entry of $P$ in the formal exposure expansion <a href=\"#eq:original-1\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:original-1\">[eq:original-1]</a> has absolute value at most $1+o(1)$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lemma-local-laws-implications",
        "exact_quote": "At a current early or cleanup state, assume $l\\ge l_0$, the core bound <a href=\"#eq:original-3\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:original-3\">[eq:original-3]</a>, and actual girth at least $g_0$. Assume positivity and <a href=\"#eq:original-8\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:original-8\">[eq:original-8]</a> at every tracked primary and auxiliary parameter, for both signs. For $B_h^+=\\mathcal D+h^{-2}\\alpha I$, the positive operator $S_h=(B_h^+)^{1/2}W(B_h^+)^{1/2}$ has norm at most $n^{v_*}/\\alpha$. For a sufficiently large fixed $C$, the sum of its eigenvalues above $Cn^c/\\alpha$ is at most $n^{v_*}/\\alpha$. These bounds hold at the primaries and their auxiliaries. At all these points, every global coordinate or centered stub $f$ satisfies $$f^TW(\\mathcal D+h^{-2}\\alpha I)Wf\n   \\le(1+h^{-2})\\|f_\\perp\\|^2+n^{-c_1}.\n \\label{eq:original-10}$$ At the primaries, the averaged trace identity is $$\\frac{\\operatorname{Tr}K_{\\rm all}}l+\n       h^{-2}\\alpha\\frac{\\mathcal T}{l}\n       =1+h^{-2}+O(n^{-c_2}).\n \\label{eq:original-11}$$ The following moment bounds hold at both primaries and auxiliaries: $$\\begin{split}\n \\operatorname{Tr}K_{\\rm all}^2+\n       \\alpha^2\\operatorname{Tr}Q_{\\rm all}^2\n   &\\le C\\left(n^{2c}l/\\alpha^2+n^{4v_*}/\\alpha^4\\right),\\\\\n \\operatorname{Tr}(\\Gamma K_{\\rm all})+\n       \\alpha\\operatorname{Tr}(\\Gamma Q_{\\rm all})\n   &\\le C\\left(n^cl/\\alpha+n^{3v_*}/\\alpha^3\\right),\\\\\n \\|Q_{\\rm all}\\|&\\le Cn^{2v_*}/\\alpha^3.\n \\end{split}\n \\label{eq:original-12}$$ The decay exponents $c_1,c_2>0$ are fixed independently of $n$.",
        "source_id": "PM-178.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-early-phase",
        "exact_quote": "For the fixed parameters of the construction and all sufficiently large $n$, the simultaneous discounted-account rule can be run from the empty pairing down to $l_1$ unresolved stubs. It retains the structural estimates <a href=\"#eq:original-3\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:original-3\">[eq:original-3]</a>–<a href=\"#eq:original-7\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:original-7\">[eq:original-7]</a>, positivity of every tracked precision, and, at every tracked point, $$|L_1|\\le n^{.075a}/\\alpha_*,\\qquad\n |L_0|\\le n^{.105a}/\\alpha_*^2,\\qquad\n \\max_{i,j}|E_{ij}|\\le n^{.33a}/\\sqrt{l_0}.$$ The low-square estimates <a href=\"#eq:original-23\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:original-23\">[eq:original-23]</a> hold with any prescribed sufficiently small fixed loss exponent, for any fixed near radius needed in the proof. At every primary point, $$\\mathcal T \\ \\ge\\ n^{-\\theta/2}(n^{-1}+\\alpha/l)^{-1}\n\\label{eq:original-13}$$ after a fixed constant is allowed in the right-hand side.",
        "source_id": "PM-178.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-early-phase",
          "exact_quote": "For the fixed parameters of the construction and all sufficiently large $n$, the simultaneous discounted-account rule can be run from the empty pairing down to $l_1$ unresolved stubs. At every primary point, $$\\mathcal T \\ \\ge\\ n^{-\\theta/2}(n^{-1}+\\alpha/l)^{-1}\n\\label{eq:original-13}$$ after a fixed constant is allowed in the right-hand side.",
          "source_id": "PM-178.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-early-pair-contractions",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A,C$ be centered $l$-by-$l$ matrices, with $A$ symmetric, and let a subscript $p$ denote their $\\{u,v\\}$ blocks. For a full uniform ordered distinct pair, $$\\begin{split}\n \\mathbb E\\operatorname{Tr}(H_0A_p)&=0,\\\\\n \\mathbb E\\operatorname{Tr}(BA_p)&=\\frac{2\\operatorname{Tr}A}{l-1},\\\\\n \\mathbb E\\operatorname{Tr}(FA_pFC_p)\n &=\\frac{2}{l(l-1)}\\left(\n       \\operatorname{Tr}A\\operatorname{Tr}C+\n       \\operatorname{Tr}(AC)-2\\sum_x A_{xx}C_{xx}\\right).\n\\end{split}$$ The last identity remains valid when $C$ is not symmetric. For a nonnegative summand, restriction to valid pairs or to survival costs a factor $1+O((D_g+1)/l)$. For a signed summand one must also subtract the omitted pairs before applying this factor.",
        "source_id": "PM-178.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-early-pair-contractions",
          "exact_quote": "Let $A,C$ be centered $l$-by-$l$ matrices, with $A$ symmetric, and let a subscript $p$ denote their $\\{u,v\\}$ blocks. For a nonnegative summand, restriction to valid pairs or to survival costs a factor $1+O((D_g+1)/l)$. For a signed summand one must also subtract the omitted pairs before applying this factor.",
          "source_id": "PM-178.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-early-index-words",
        "exact_quote": "Expand every occurrence of $H_0$ as $-\\sigma h F-\\sigma hJ_2/(l-2)$, every intermediate factor as $T+D_E$, and each outer factor as $P+E$. A resulting scalar word has two outer endpoint indices $s,t\\in\\{u,v\\}$, alternating swap and intermediate factors, and one of the following descriptions.*\n\n1.  *It contains a non-pure swap $-\\sigma hJ_2/(l-2)$.*\n\n2.  *All swaps are pure and it contains an off-diagonal $T$ factor, bounded by $b_{uv}$.*\n\n3.  *All swaps are pure, all $T$ factors are diagonal, and at least two intermediate factors are $D_E$.*\n\n4.  *All swaps are pure, all $T$ factors are diagonal, there is at least one intermediate factor, and there is at most one $D_E$ factor. If $s=t$, either there is a diagonal $T$ factor at $\\bar s$, or the word has exactly one intermediate factor, namely $(D_E)_{\\bar s\\bar s}$. If $s\\ne t$ and there is a $T$ factor, there is at least one local factor $j_u$ or $j_v$. If there is no $D_E$ factor, both local factors occur.*\n\n5.  *There are no intermediate factors. Its pure-swap portion has $s\\ne t$.*\n\n*Here $\\bar u=v$ and $\\bar v=u$. The list is exhaustive; the cases are made disjoint by testing them in the displayed order. With only one intermediate $D_E$ and no $T$, the exceptional same-end word in item 4 is precisely $h^2 x_i(s)x_j(s)e_{\\bar s}(\\bar s)$, where $x_i,x_j$ are the chosen outer arrays, each a $p$ or $e$ row. All estimates obtained by retaining at most a fixed number of marked factors in these cases can be summed over the remaining words with a constant independent of $n$.",
        "source_id": "PM-178.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-early-entry-mean",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-early-near-reference",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $k$. For $i\\in X$, $u\\in S$ at actual vertex distance at most $k$, remove from the actual part of $p_i(u)$ the monomial of the unique short path. The squared sum of the remaining coefficientwise absolute majorants over all these pairs is at most $$n^\\eta+C(1+L)^4q^L/l.$$ Here $X=V$ or $S$, and $\\eta$ can be any prescribed positive constant after the structural losses are reduced.",
        "source_id": "PM-178.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-early-near-reference",

          "source_id": "PM-178.1"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-early-low-squares",
        "exact_quote": "For any fixed radius $R_c$, the simultaneous accounts retain $$\\begin{split}\n &T_S=\\sum_{x\\in S}e_x(x)^2\\le n^\\eta,\n \\qquad T_V=\\sum_{i\\in V}E_{ii}^2\\le n^\\eta n/l_0,\\\\\n &T_{XS,k}=\\sum_{\\substack{i\\in X,\\ x\\in S\\\\\n    \\operatorname{dist}(\\operatorname{vtx}i,\\operatorname{vtx}x)\\le k}}\n           e_i(x)^2\\le n^\\eta,\n \\qquad X\\in\\{V,S\\},\\quad 0\\le k\\le R_c .\n\\end{split}\n\\label{eq:original-23}$$ The final exponent $\\eta>0$ may be prescribed in advance and made arbitrarily small.",
        "source_id": "PM-178.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-early-low-squares",

          "source_id": "PM-178.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "For any fixed radius $R_c$, the simultaneous accounts retain $$\\begin{split}\n &T_S=\\sum_{x\\in S}e_x(x)^2\\le n^\\eta,\n \\qquad T_V=\\sum_{i\\in V}E_{ii}^2\\le n^\\eta n/l_0,\\\\\n &T_{XS,k}=\\sum_{\\substack{i\\in X,\\ x\\in S\\\\\n    \\operatorname{dist}(\\operatorname{vtx}i,\\operatorname{vtx}x)\\le k}}\n           e_i(x)^2\\le n^\\eta,\n \\qquad X\\in\\{V,S\\},\\quad 0\\le k\\le R_c .\n\\end{split}\n\\label{eq:original-23}$$ The final exponent $\\eta>0$ may be prescribed in advance and made arbitrarily small.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-early-interpolation",
        "exact_quote": "Let a measure of bounded mass satisfy $\\int|z|^2\\le n^{-\\beta}$ and $\\int|z|^p\\le M$. For every fixed $0<t<p$, $$\\int|z|^t\\le n^{-\\beta_t}(1+M)^{t/p},\n \\qquad \\beta_t>0,\n\\label{eq:original-28}$$ for sufficiently large $n$. If a nonnegative kernel $K$ on two probability spaces has both marginal densities at most $b$, then for nonnegative $f,g$ and $r,s\\ge0$, $r+s\\le1$, $$\\iint K(x,y)f(x)^rg(y)^s\\,dx\\,dy\n \\le b\\left(\\int f\\right)^r\\left(\\int g\\right)^s.$$ For $K(i,u)=|p_i(u)|^k$, $2\\le k\\le p$, with uniform $i\\in X,u\\in S$, one may use $b=n^{\\eta_0}/l$ for any fixed $\\eta_0>0$, once $n$ is sufficiently large. The row marginal alone is at most $C_p/l$. For $k=p$ or $2p$, the constants in the row and column sums can be bounded by a constant depending only on $d$, for each fixed $p$ and large $n$.",
        "source_id": "PM-178.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-early-interpolation",

If a nonnegative kernel $K$ on two probability spaces has both marginal densities at most $b$, then for nonnegative $f,g$ and $r,s\\ge0$, $r+s\\le1$, $$\\iint K(x,y)f(x)^rg(y)^s\\,dx\\,dy\n \\le b\\left(\\int f\\right)^r\\left(\\int g\\right)^s.$$ For $K(i,u)=|p_i(u)|^k$, $2\\le k\\le p$, with uniform $i\\in X,u\\in S$, one may use $b=n^{\\eta_0}/l$ for any fixed $\\eta_0>0$, once $n$ is sufficiently large. The row marginal alone is at most $C_p/l$. For $k=p$ or $2p$, the constants in the row and column sums can be bounded by a constant depending only on $d$, for each fixed $p$ and large $n$.",
          "source_id": "PM-178.1"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-early-linearization",
        "exact_quote": "For every surviving output pair, decompose its actual increment as $\\Delta E_{ij}=B_e\\mathcal L_{ij}+\\mathcal N_{ij}$, where $\\mathcal L$ is linear in $z$ and evaluated on the old output vectors. The residual contains the nonlinear terms, the formal tail, and the recentering of surviving stub outputs. It has sup $$|\\mathcal N_{ij}|\\le C(d_m^2+B_{\\rm tail}+d_m/l)$$ and conditional second moment $$\\mathbb E|\\mathcal N_{ij}|^2\n \\le C(n^\\eta/l^2+d_m^4/l).\n\\label{eq:original-29}$$ The leading terms of $\\mathcal L_{ij}$, up to their signs, are $$h p_i(u)z_{vj},\\quad h p_i(v)z_{uj},\\quad\n h p_j(u)z_{iv},\\quad h p_j(v)z_{iu},\n\\label{eq:original-30}$$ and $$h^2p_i(s)p_j(t)z_{\\bar s\\bar t},\n \\qquad s,t\\in\\{u,v\\}.\n\\label{eq:original-31}$$ All other coefficients have sup bounded by a fixed negative power of $n$. A diagonal coefficient of $H_P$ satisfies, with its first endpoint $s$ fixed, $$\\mathbb E_{\\bar s}\\bigl[|(H_P)_{ss}|^k\n                  \\mathbf 1_{\\rm valid}\\bigr]\n \\le C_p n^\\eta n^{-c'_0(k-2)}/l,\n \\qquad 2\\le k\\le p,\n\\label{eq:original-32}$$ where the expectation may also omit boundedly many output labels.",
        "source_id": "PM-178.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-early-linearization",
          "exact_quote": "For every surviving output pair, decompose its actual increment as $\\Delta E_{ij}=B_e\\mathcal L_{ij}+\\mathcal N_{ij}$, where $\\mathcal L$ is linear in $z$ and evaluated on the old output vectors. The residual contains the nonlinear terms, the formal tail, and the recentering of surviving stub outputs. It has sup $$|\\mathcal N_{ij}|\\le C(d_m^2+B_{\\rm tail}+d_m/l)$$ and conditional second moment $$\\mathbb E|\\mathcal N_{ij}|^2\n \\le C(n^\\eta/l^2+d_m^4/l).\n\\label{eq:original-29}$$ The leading terms of $\\mathcal L_{ij}$, up to their signs, are $$h p_i(u)z_{vj},\\quad h p_i(v)z_{uj},\\quad\n h p_j(u)z_{iv},\\quad h p_j(v)z_{iu},\n\\label{eq:original-30}$$ and $$h^2p_i(s)p_j(t)z_{\\bar s\\bar t},\n \\qquad s,t\\in\\{u,v\\}.\n\\label{eq:original-31}$$ All other coefficients have sup bounded by a fixed negative power of $n$. A diagonal coefficient of $H_P$ satisfies, with its first endpoint $s$ fixed, $$\\mathbb E_{\\bar s}\\bigl[|(H_P)_{ss}|^k\n                  \\mathbf 1_{\\rm valid}\\bigr]\n \\le C_p n^\\eta n^{-c'_0(k-2)}/l,\n \\qquad 2\\le k\\le p,\n\\label{eq:original-32}$$ where the expectation may also omit boundedly many output labels.",
          "source_id": "PM-178.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "For every surviving output pair, decompose its actual increment as $\\Delta E_{ij}=B_e\\mathcal L_{ij}+\\mathcal N_{ij}$, where $\\mathcal L$ is linear in $z$ and evaluated on the old output vectors. It has sup $$|\\mathcal N_{ij}|\\le C(d_m^2+B_{\\rm tail}+d_m/l)$$ and conditional second moment $$\\mathbb E|\\mathcal N_{ij}|^2\n \\le C(n^\\eta/l^2+d_m^4/l).\n\\label{eq:original-29}$$ The leading terms of $\\mathcal L_{ij}$, up to their signs, are $$h p_i(u)z_{vj},\\quad h p_i(v)z_{uj},\\quad\n h p_j(u)z_{iv},\\quad h p_j(v)z_{iu},\n\\label{eq:original-30}$$ and $$h^2p_i(s)p_j(t)z_{\\bar s\\bar t},\n \\qquad s,t\\in\\{u,v\\}.\n\\label{eq:original-31}$$ All other coefficients have sup bounded by a fixed negative power of $n$. A diagonal coefficient of $H_P$ satisfies, with its first endpoint $s$ fixed, $$\\mathbb E_{\\bar s}\\bigl[|(H_P)_{ss}|^k\n                  \\mathbf 1_{\\rm valid}\\bigr]\n \\le C_p n^\\eta n^{-c'_0(k-2)}/l,\n \\qquad 2\\le k\\le p,\n\\label{eq:original-32}$$ where the expectation may also omit boundedly many output labels.",
      "explanation": "Separates the old-axis linear entry update from nonlinear errors, the formal truncation tail and recentering. The coefficient bounds identify which same-end terms gain a conditional moment saving.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-early-high-moments",
        "exact_quote": "There is a constant $C_*$, independent of every sufficiently large even order $p$, with the following property. After $p$ is fixed, one may choose a fixed $\\kappa_p>0$, a fixed near radius $R_c$, and sufficiently small structural and low-square losses such that $$Z_p=1+F_p+\\kappa_pD_p^{\\rm mom},\\qquad\n \\mathbb E Z_p'\\le(1+C_*/l+u_l)Z_p,\n \\qquad \\sum_lu_l=o(1),\n\\label{eq:original-33}$$ with nonnegative deterministic upper budgets $u_l$ under the bootstrap assumptions.",
        "source_id": "PM-178.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-early-high-moments",
          "exact_quote": "There is a constant $C_*$, independent of every sufficiently large even order $p$, with the following property.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-cleanup",
        "exact_quote": "Assume the early-phase estimates at $l_1$, and maintain the structural estimators during cleanup. With the simultaneous discounted-account choice rule, the cleanup interval can be completed so that, at every chosen state, the precision matrices remain positive definite, <a href=\"#eq:original-8\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:original-8\">[eq:original-8]</a> holds, and $$T_S=\\sum_{x\\in S}e_x(x)^2\\leq n^\\eta,\\qquad\n |E_{ij}|\\leq2B_k.$$ The second inequality holds for every surviving pair $(i,j)$ of total level $k=\\nu(i)+\\nu(j)$. At primary points the lower estimate for $\\mathcal T$ persists in the form $$\\mathcal T\\geq n^{-\\theta}(n^{-1}+\\alpha/l)^{-1}.$$ In particular the consequences <a href=\"#eq:original-10\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:original-10\">[eq:original-10]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:original-11\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:original-11\">[eq:original-11]</a>, and a polynomial-loss form of <a href=\"#eq:original-13\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:original-13\">[eq:original-13]</a>, hold at $l_0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-178.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-cleanup-words",
        "exact_quote": "After expansion of a numerical update difference and of its formal truncation tail, every term is covered by the following classes:*\n\n1.  *a term containing a non-pure swap $R$, and hence a factor $C/l$;*\n\n2.  *a pure-swap term with no intermediate endpoint factor;*\n\n3.  *a pure-swap term with exactly one endpoint $E$ factor and no topology factor;*\n\n4.  *a term with a cross topology factor $b_{uv}$;*\n\n5.  *a pure-swap term with local topology factors only, and at most one endpoint $E$ factor;*\n\n6.  *a term with at least two endpoint $E$ factors.*\n\n*In class 3 the single endpoint $E$ factor may be grouped with $W$ outers. For class 5, if both outers are at $u$, an opposite local factor $j_v$ is necessary; the symmetric statement holds for two outers at $v$. If class 5 has no endpoint $E$ factor and has distinct outers, local factors at both endpoints are necessary. All remaining factors and choices of their positions can be summed with a bounded geometric multiplier.",
        "source_id": "PM-178.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cleanup-words",
          "exact_quote": "After expansion of a numerical update difference and of its formal truncation tail, every term is covered by the following classes:*\n\n1.  *a term containing a non-pure swap $R$, and hence a factor $C/l$;*\n\n2.  *a pure-swap term with no intermediate endpoint factor;*\n\n3.  *a pure-swap term with exactly one endpoint $E$ factor and no topology factor;*\n\n4.  *a term with a cross topology factor $b_{uv}$;*\n\n5.  *a pure-swap term with local topology factors only, and at most one endpoint $E$ factor;*\n\n6.  *a term with at least two endpoint $E$ factors.*\n\n*In class 3 the single endpoint $E$ factor may be grouped with $W$ outers. For class 5, if both outers are at $u$, an opposite local factor $j_v$ is necessary; the symmetric statement holds for two outers at $v$. If class 5 has no endpoint $E$ factor and has distinct outers, local factors at both endpoints are necessary. All remaining factors and choices of their positions can be summed with a bounded geometric multiplier.",
          "source_id": "PM-178.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-boundary-completion",
        "exact_quote": "Under these inputs, the account rule can deterministically complete the pairing. Every added edge is simple and preserves the girth exclusion. At the end the precision is positive at every primary point for both signs. There is a reference matrix $\\widehat P_{\\rm fin}$, defined below by exact numerical updates from a smooth coefficient truncation at the stop, such that at every retained primary parameter and both signs, $$\\max_{i,j}|(W_{\\rm fin}-\\widehat P_{\\rm fin})_{ij}|\n \\le C B_B,\n \\qquad B_B=\\frac{n^{.40a}}{\\sqrt{l_2}}.$$ The construction retains the stop configuration and the matching added after it. These define the same family $\\widehat P_{\\rm fin}(h)$ throughout the primary interval. All the accounts, candidate values and transitions in this section can be evaluated in a number of bit operations polynomial in $n$, with an exponent depending only on the fixed parameters.",
        "source_id": "PM-178.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-boundary-completion",
          "exact_quote": "Under these inputs, the account rule can deterministically complete the pairing. Every added edge is simple and preserves the girth exclusion. These define the same family $\\widehat P_{\\rm fin}(h)$ throughout the primary interval. All the accounts, candidate values and transitions in this section can be evaluated in a number of bit operations polynomial in $n$, with an exponent depending only on the fixed parameters.",
          "source_id": "PM-178.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Under these inputs, the account rule can deterministically complete the pairing. Every added edge is simple and preserves the girth exclusion. At the end the precision is positive at every primary point for both signs. There is a reference matrix $\\widehat P_{\\rm fin}$, defined below by exact numerical updates from a smooth coefficient truncation at the stop, such that at every retained primary parameter and both signs, $$\\max_{i,j}|(W_{\\rm fin}-\\widehat P_{\\rm fin})_{ij}|\n \\le C B_B,\n \\qquad B_B=\\frac{n^{.40a}}{\\sqrt{l_2}}.$$ The construction retains the stop configuration and the matching added after it. These define the same family $\\widehat P_{\\rm fin}(h)$ throughout the primary interval. All the accounts, candidate values and transitions in this section can be evaluated in a number of bit operations polynomial in $n$, with an exponent depending only on the fixed parameters.",
      "explanation": "Completes the separated boundary with simple edges and signed positive precisions. It retains a single smoothly parameterized reference determined by the stop configuration and the completed matching.",

        }

        "exact_quote": "There are constants $r_P'>0$ and $C$ such that $$\\widehat\\Gamma_0:=V_0^T\\widehat P_0V_0\n   =I+\\widehat D_0\\quad\\hbox{on }\\mathcal H_0,\n \\qquad \\|\\widehat D_0\\|\\le \\frac{C}{R_0}.\n \\label{eq:original-40}$$ For every global vertex coordinate $i$, the vector $p_{i,0}=V_0^T\\widehat P_0e_i$ satisfies $$p_{i,0}=c_i\\Pi_{l_0}e_{s(i)}+\\tau_i,\n \\qquad |c_i|\\le1,\\qquad \\|\\tau_i\\|_2\\le n^{-r_P'}.\n \\label{eq:original-41}$$ The first summand is omitted if there is no actual path from $i$ to a boundary vertex of length less than $\\lfloor R_0/4\\rfloor$. Otherwise that vertex and that path are unique, and $c_i=(\\sigma h)^{\\operatorname{dist}(i,s(i))}$. For a boundary vertex, $s(i)$ is its own stub and $c_i=1$. The estimate for $\\tau_i$ holds also for $\\partial_h\\tau_i$, after decreasing $r_P'$ if necessary.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-boundary-pair-averages",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that $D\\mathbf1=0$, $\\max_x(D^2)_{xx}\\le C_0$ and $\\max_{x,y}|D_{xy}|\\le\\epsilon=o(1)$. Then $\\|H\\|\\le C$, and $$|H_{uu}|\\le C(l^{-1}+|D_{vv}|+|D_{uv}|).$$ The conditional absolute mean of $(H-H_0)_{uu}$ with $u$ fixed is at most $C(1+\\sqrt T)/\\sqrt l$, and the conditional second moment of $H_{uu}$ is at most $C(1+T)/l$. The kernel $|(H-H_0)_{uv}|$ has every row and column sum at most $C(1+\\sqrt T)\\sqrt l$. After subtracting the single-$A$ term $-H_0AH_0$, these absolute bounds gain a factor $C\\epsilon$. All these statements remain valid if the possible partner set omits a fixed number of coordinates.*\n\n*If $x,y$ are arbitrary scalar arrays and $\\mathcal I$ is the allowed endpoint set, $m=|\\mathcal I|$, then the pure-swap single-$A$ contraction has the exact formula $$\\mathbb E_{\\mathcal I}\n     [(I^Tx)^TF A F(I^Ty)]\n =\\frac{2}{m(m-1)}\\left[\n   Y_{\\mathcal I}\\langle x_{\\mathcal I},y_{\\mathcal I}\\rangle\n  +x_{\\mathcal I}^TD_{\\mathcal I\\mathcal I}y_{\\mathcal I}\n  -2\\sum_{u\\in\\mathcal I}D_{uu}x_uy_u\\right],$$ where $Y_{\\mathcal I}=\\sum_{u\\in\\mathcal I}D_{uu}$. For full pairs the following additional identities hold: $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb E\\operatorname{Tr}(F A F K)\n &=\\frac{2}{l(l-1)}\n    \\left[YX+\\operatorname{Tr}D^3\n                 -2\\sum_uD_{uu}(D^2)_{uu}\\right],\\\\\n \\mathbb E\\operatorname{Tr}(F K F K)\n &=\\frac{2}{l(l-1)}\n    \\left[X^2+\\operatorname{Tr}D^4\n                 -2\\sum_u(D^2)_{uu}^2\\right],\\\\\n \\mathbb E[(I^Tr)^TF K F(I^Tr)]\n &=\\frac{2}{l(l-1)}\n    \\left[gX+r^TD^2r-2\\sum_ur_u^2(D^2)_{uu}\\right],\n \\qquad g=\\|r\\|^2.\n\\end{aligned}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-178.1"
      },

For full pairs the following additional identities hold: $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb E\\operatorname{Tr}(F A F K)\n &=\\frac{2}{l(l-1)}\n    \\left[YX+\\operatorname{Tr}D^3\n                 -2\\sum_uD_{uu}(D^2)_{uu}\\right],\\\\\n \\mathbb E\\operatorname{Tr}(F K F K)\n &=\\frac{2}{l(l-1)}\n    \\left[X^2+\\operatorname{Tr}D^4\n                 -2\\sum_u(D^2)_{uu}^2\\right],\\\\\n \\mathbb E[(I^Tr)^TF K F(I^Tr)]\n &=\\frac{2}{l(l-1)}\n    \\left[gX+r^TD^2r-2\\sum_ur_u^2(D^2)_{uu}\\right],\n \\qquad g=\\|r\\|^2.\n\\end{aligned}$$",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-repair-projector",
        "exact_quote": "Assume $x_i^\\sigma>-e/20$ for all nonconstant indices $i$ and both signs. Suppose the final resolvents $R_\\sigma(z)=(z-\\sigma A_G)^{-1}|_{\\mathbf 1^\\perp}$ at the primary points $z=z(h)$ satisfy the entry comparison $$\\max_p\\left|\n R_\\sigma(z(h))_{pp}-m_h\\widehat P_{\\mathrm{fin},pp}^\\sigma(h)\n \\right|\\le C B_B,\n \\qquad B_B=o(\\sqrt e).$$ Suppose also that the same frozen reference is defined throughout the primary range and obeys $$\\sup_{h,p}\\left|\n \\frac{d}{dh}\\bigl(m_h\\widehat P_{\\mathrm{fin},pp}^\\sigma(h)\\bigr)\n \\right|\\le C.$$ Assume the primary grid has bounded geometric spacing, as in the construction. Then, uniformly in $p$ and $\\sigma$, $$\\sum_{i:\\,x_i^\\sigma\\le w}|v_i(p)|^2\\le Cw^{3/2}\n \\qquad (2e\\le w\\le c_d).\n \\label{eq:original-61}$$ The constant can be enlarged to make the same assertion valid for all larger $w$ in the bounded spectral range.",
