        "anchor": "stmt-lem-repair-bulk",
        "exact_quote": "The projector estimate <a href=\"#eq:original-61\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:original-61\">[eq:original-61]</a> implies $$\\sup_p\\bigl[(R_B^\\sigma)^2\\bigr]_{pp}\n \\le \\frac{C}{\\sqrt{W_b}}.\n \\label{eq:original-63}$$ If $w_p\\asymp W_b$ is a primary gap, every entry of $R_B^\\sigma-R_\\sigma(s+w_p)$ is $O(\\sqrt{W_b})$.*\n\n*For the typical-edge assertion below, the additional inputs are the following specific conclusions at the separation stop:*\n\n1.  *Only $O(l_0)$ edges of $G$ were added after the stop, with $l_0\\asymp n^{2/3+.15a}$. At most $n^{.08+o(1)}$ stop vertices have a nonempty actual nonbacktracking return of length at most $r_n=\\lfloor .08\\log_q n\\rfloor+2$.*\n\n2.  *The number of actual nonbacktracking paths of any length $k<L$ between fixed endpoints is bounded by a constant. The stop reference is the weighted sum of these paths with coefficient $\\varphi(k/L)(\\sigma h)^k$, up to a matrix whose entries are $o(n^{-.04})$. The taper is bounded and equals one for $k\\le r_n$, and $h\\le h_c$.*\n\n3.  *The final reference has the form $\\widehat P_{\\mathrm{fin}}=\\widehat P_0-\n     \\widehat P_0V_0H_fV_0^T\\widehat P_0$, where $V_0$ is an isometry from an $(l_0-1)$-dimensional space, $\\|H_f\\|\\le C$, and $\\|\\widehat P_0\\|\\le C(1+L)^3$. At the comparison primary points the final resolvent has entry error $O(B_B)=o(n^{-.04})$ relative to $m_h\\widehat P_{\\mathrm{fin}}$.*\n\n*The return count, multiplicity and virtual-path assertions are consequences of Lemmas <a href=\"#lemma:structure\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lemma:structure\">7</a> and <a href=\"#lemma:path-majorants\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lemma:path-majorants\">8</a>; the norm bound is Lemma <a href=\"#lemma:pencil-coefficients\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lemma:pencil-coefficients\">9</a>; the reference representation and final error are furnished by Section <a href=\"#sec:boundary-completion\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:boundary-completion\">7</a> and Proposition <a href=\"#prop:boundary-completion\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:boundary-completion\">29</a>. All inputs are uniform in the primary points used below. For each pair of cutoffs as above, all but a fraction $O(n^{-.1})$ of the oriented edges $(p,p')$ have, for both signs, their endpoint block of $R_B^\\sigma$ within $n^{-.024}$ of $P_{\\mathrm{tree}}^\\sigma$. The estimates are uniform in the cutoffs; their exceptional sets may depend on the cutoffs.",

Let $v_2,\\ldots,v_n$ be an orthonormal eigenbasis of $A_G$ on $\\mathbf1^\\perp$, with eigenvalues $\\lambda_i$, and put $x_i^\\sigma=s-\\sigma\\lambda_i$ for $\\sigma\\in\\{+1,-1\\}$. Assume the following three properties.*\n\n1.  **Spectral confinement.* For every $i=2,\\ldots,n$ and both signs, $x_i^\\sigma>-e/20$.*\n\n2.  **Local spectral mass.* For every vertex $p$, both signs, and every $w\\in[2e,2d]$, $$\\sum_{\\substack{2\\le i\\le n\\\\x_i^\\sigma\\le w}}|v_i(p)|^2\n     \\le C_0w^{3/2}.$$*\n\n3.  **Typical edge blocks.* For every pair $w_B^\\sigma\\in[W_b,2W_b]\\setminus\\{x_i^\\sigma:2\\le i\\le n\\}$, define $$R_B^\\sigma=\\sum_{\\substack{2\\le i\\le n\\\\x_i^\\sigma\\ge w_B^\\sigma}}\n                     \\frac{v_iv_i^T}{x_i^\\sigma}.$$ All but a fraction $C_0n^{-.1}$ of the $dn$ oriented edges $(p,p')$ satisfy the following bound for both signs: $$\\left\\|\n     \\begin{pmatrix}(R_B^\\sigma)_{pp}&(R_B^\\sigma)_{pp'}\\\\\n                     (R_B^\\sigma)_{p'p}&(R_B^\\sigma)_{p'p'}\\end{pmatrix}\n     -\\frac1{d-2}\\begin{pmatrix}\\sqrt q&\\sigma\\\\\\sigma&\\sqrt q\\end{pmatrix}\n     \\right\\|_{\\max}\\le n^{-.024}.$$ Here $\\|\\cdot\\|_{\\max}$ is the largest absolute entry. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Nonconstant adjacency spectrum** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The restriction $A_G\|_{\mathbf1^\perp}$ measures the remaining spectrum.
- **Stub pairing** ([source](#pm-h-5)): Give each vertex $d$ distinguishable half-edges, called stubs.
- **Valid edge switch** ([source](#pm-h-4)): An edge switch deletes $\{u,v\}$ and $\{x,y\}$ and inserts $\{u,y\}$ and $\{x,v\}$. It is valid when the four endpoints are distinct and the inserted edges were absent.
- **Truncated path reference** ([source](#pm-h-7)): The first reference, denoted by $P$, is a finite weighted sum of nonbacktracking paths, with degrees below the cutoff $L$ fixed below.
- **Early and cleanup entry error** ([source](#pm-h-7)): Through cleanup the entry error is $E=W-P$.
- **Continued smooth reference and entry error** ([source](#pm-h-7)): At the separated boundary we replace this path sum by a smooth truncation $\widehat P_0$ and continue it by exact inverse updates, obtaining $\widehat P$; the error is then $E=W-\widehat P$.
- **Centered unresolved-stub incidence** ([source](#pm-h-13)): For $l>0$, let $C_S$ be the vertex–stub incidence matrix, and put $$t=C_S\mathbf 1,\qquad \Pi_l=I-\frac{\mathbf 1\mathbf 1^T}{l},\qquad \Xi=C_S\Pi_l,\qquad \mathcal D=\Xi\Xi^T.$$
- **Partial-configuration precision and normalized inverse** ([source](#pm-h-13)): If $A$ is the adjacency matrix of the resolved pairs, define $$A_e=A+\frac{tt^T}{l},\qquad M(h)=z(h)I-\sigma A_e-h\mathcal D,\qquad m_h=\frac{h}{1-h^2},\qquad W=M(h)^{-1}/m_h.$$
- **Formal path truncation error** ([source](#eq:original-1)): Let $P=P_{<L}$ be the degree-$<L$ truncation of this generating function, and write $E=W-P$.
- **Signed spectral slack** ([source](#sec:exact-repair)): Choose an orthonormal eigenbasis $v_2,\ldots,v_n$ of $\mathbf 1^\perp$ for the adjacency matrix $A_G$, and write $$A_Gv_i=\lambda_i v_i,\qquad x_i^\sigma=s-\sigma\lambda_i \quad (\sigma\in\{+1,-1\}).$$
- **Near subspace and truncated bulk reciprocal** ([source](#pm-h-52)): For a cutoff $w_B^\sigma\in[W_b,2W_b]$ avoiding the signed slacks $\{x_i^\sigma:2\le i\le n\}$, define $$E_0^\sigma=\operatorname{span}\{v_i:x_i^\sigma<w_B^\sigma\}, \qquad R_B^\sigma=\sum_{x_i^\sigma\ge w_B^\sigma} \frac{v_iv_i^T}{x_i^\sigma}.$$
- **Rational contrast basis** ([source](#sec:algorithm)): For each simple regular candidate, take the explicit rational contrast basis $$B=[e_1-e_n,\ldots,e_{n-1}-e_n].$$

|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-tree-spectral-threshold | Tree spectral threshold | = | $2\sqrt{d-1}$ | Fixed regular degree. |
| q-main-bit-cost | Full adjacency-list construction bit cost | ≤ | $O_d(n^{k_d})$ | The degree is fixed; the exponent and threshold may depend on it. |
| q-target-order-upper-bound | Output vertex count for a requested target | ≤ | $N+1$ | Fixed degree and sufficiently large requested order. |
| q-base-exponent | Base exponent | = | $10^{-4}$ | A fixed exponent independent of graph order. |
| q-spectral-error-scale | Spectral error scale | = | $n^{-2/3+a}$ | Fixed degree and base exponent; sufficiently large graph order. |
| q-bulk-cutoff-scale | Bulk slack cutoff scale | = | $n^{-2/3+100a}$ | Fixed repair parameters and sufficiently large order. |
| q-switch-collection-size | Number of vertex-disjoint repair switches | = | $\lceil n^{1/3+10a}\rceil$ | The degree and local-estimate constant are fixed. |
| q-final-reference-error-scale | Final reference comparison scale | = | $\frac{n^{.40a}}{\sqrt{l_2}}$ | Fixed construction parameters and sufficiently large even order. |
| q-local-edge-mass | Local spectral mass at a signed edge | ≤ | $Cw^{3/2}$ | The source slack interval begins above the confinement error; larger bounded slacks are covered by enlarging the constant. |
| q-bulk-reciprocal-row-square | Squared bulk-resolvent diagonal | ≤ | $\frac{C}{\sqrt{W_b}}$ | A spectrum-avoiding cutoff in the prescribed bulk interval and either sign. |
| q-typical-edge-error | Typical endpoint-block comparison error | ≤ | $n^{-.024}$ | Both signs for each fixed pair of admissible cutoffs; the exceptional set may vary with that pair. |
| q-bernstein-approximation-degree | Permitted spectral-function approximation degree | = | $\lceil n^{2(c+C)}\rceil$ | Fixed Lipschitz and accuracy exponents. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-discounted-selection** [claim](#stmt-lem-discounted-accounts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-discounted-accounts): Finite weighted averaging and account-specific discount products. -> A minimizing transition preserves the common discounted total.
- **step-scalar-excess** [claim](#stmt-lem-scalar-excess) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-scalar-excess): Taylor expansion of the positive-part power with drift and variance bounds. -> An expectation-growth bound for a scalar excess account.
- **step-prescribed-scale** [claim](#stmt-lem-positive-excess-account) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-positive-excess-account): Rescale the scalar excess estimate and apply it to each sign. -> Two-sided positive-excess control at the prescribed scale.
- **step-configuration-algebra** [claim](#stmt-lemma-configuration-algebra) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-configuration-algebra): Nonbacktracking recurrence, direct exposure algebra, Woodbury updates and Schur-complement inertia. -> Formal path coefficients and exact exposure identities with conditional positivity preservation.
- **step-structural-estimators** [claim](#stmt-lemma-structure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-structure): Colorful completion estimators, bounded-width dynamic programming and rejection-discount estimates. -> Core bounds, a small bad-stub set and cleanup separation.
- **step-path-majorants** [claim](#stmt-lemma-path-majorants) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-path-majorants): Absolute coefficient majorization, stopped-walk row bounds and structural path diagrams. -> Row sums, squared sums and admissible-pair decay.
- **step-pencil-coefficients** [claim](#stmt-lemma-pencil-coefficients) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-pencil-coefficients): Scalar pencil root exclusion, Cauchy coefficient bounds and absolute path estimates. -> Operator, signed-column and absolute-column bounds for the path truncation.
- **step-resolvent-consequences** [claim](#stmt-lemma-local-laws-implications) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-local-laws-implications): Positive finite-difference filtering, ordered divided differences and resolvent comparison. -> Current-state norm, tail, local-energy, averaged-trace and moment estimates.
- **step-pair-contractions** [claim](#stmt-lem-early-pair-contractions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-early-pair-contractions): Expand pair-block traces and sum centered ordered-distinct entries exactly. -> Signed contraction identities and correct restriction rules.
- **step-trace-transitions** [claim](#stmt-lem-early-trace-transitions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-early-trace-transitions): Expand exact inverse updates and retain centered trace cancellations. -> Trace drift, variance and reciprocal-trace increment budgets.
- **step-trace-accounts** [claim](#stmt-lem-early-trace-accounts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-early-trace-accounts): Normalize trace-square accounts and Taylor-expand the reciprocal statistic. -> Bounded trace-account discounts with extraction slack.
- **step-index-words** [claim](#stmt-lem-early-index-words) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-early-index-words): Classify swap and intermediate-factor index words in a disjoint testing order. -> An exhaustive classification with a summable geometric remainder.
- **step-signed-entry-mean** [claim](#stmt-lem-early-entry-mean) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-early-entry-mean): Retain zero-intermediate and single-error cancellations, then bound omitted pairs and recentering. -> A survival-conditional signed mean bound for every existing entry.
- **step-near-reference** [claim](#stmt-lem-early-near-reference) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-early-near-reference): Remove the unique short monomial and count cycle-containing alternative-path diagrams. -> A squared residual bound for aligned near paths.
- **step-low-squares** [claim](#stmt-lem-early-low-squares) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-early-low-squares): Balance diagonal copying against deletion and induct over newly near pairs. -> Simultaneous diagonal and near-pair square-sum bounds.
- **step-interpolation** [claim](#stmt-lem-early-interpolation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-early-interpolation): Moment interpolation and Hölder bounds for kernels with controlled marginals. -> Power savings for mixed intermediate moments.
- **step-linearization** [claim](#stmt-lem-early-linearization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-early-linearization): Use the exact two-reference update identity and classify nonlinear and tail residuals. -> Linear leading terms and residual suprema and second moments.
- **step-high-moments** [claim](#stmt-lem-early-high-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-early-high-moments): Separate mixed and pure powers and weight diagonal moments to control transfers. -> A high-moment growth coefficient independent of the later extraction order.
- **step-early-pairing** [claim](#stmt-prop-early-phase) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-early-high-moments-2): Joint induction: define positive candidates, minimize discounted accounts and extract next-state caps. -> A sparse unresolved boundary with the early-phase estimates.
- **step-cleanup-pathwise** [claim](#stmt-lem-cleanup-pathwise) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cleanup-pathwise): Dominate earlier actual paths by the current history graph and use distinct prescribed endpoints. -> Pathwise cumulative squared majorant bounds.
- **step-cleanup-traces** [claim](#stmt-lem-cleanup-traces) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cleanup-traces): Fixed-endpoint centered contractions and deviation-square accounts. -> Persistence of scalar trace bounds and the primary lower trace estimate.
- **step-cleanup-words** [claim](#stmt-lem-cleanup-words) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cleanup-words): Classify endpoint words for a prescribed first endpoint and prove opposite-factor requirements. -> Exhaustive cleanup word classes with geometric summability.
- **step-cleanup-diagonals** [claim](#stmt-lem-cleanup-diagonal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cleanup-diagonal): Bound the vector of signed means and summed surviving increment squares. -> Cumulative budgets for the surviving diagonal-square account.
- **step-cleanup-jumps** [claim](#stmt-lem-cleanup-jumps) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cleanup-jumps): Compare label levels in the exact linear update and bound nonlinear, tail and recentering terms. -> A power-small normalized jump at every allowed surviving step.
- **step-cleanup-entry-budgets** [claim](#stmt-lem-cleanup-entry-budgets) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cleanup-entry-budgets): Retain signed partner cancellations and sum ordinary conditional budgets along each prefix. -> Power-small normalized cumulative drift and second moments for each alive entry.
- **step-cleanup-completion** [claim](#stmt-prop-cleanup) / [proof](#pm-h-32): Use alive-label positive-excess accounts with special-event comparators and simultaneous scalar extraction. -> Separated unresolved stubs and preserved analytic estimates.
- **step-smooth-reference** [claim](#stmt-lem-boundary-smooth-reference) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-boundary-smooth-reference): Unique short paths, boundary moment matching and tapered Chebyshev-kernel estimates. -> Sparse boundary rows and a small compressed reference operator.
- **step-boundary-pair-averages** [claim](#stmt-lem-boundary-pair-averages) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-boundary-pair-averages): Endpoint Neumann expansions and exact ordered-distinct contractions. -> Coefficient kernels and signed averaging identities for boundary updates.
- **step-boundary-completion** [claim](#stmt-prop-boundary-completion) / [proof](#sec:boundary-completion): Continue one frozen reference; amplify the averaged gap, contract rows, concentrate entries and perform the final matching. -> A simple regular graph with signed confinement and a retained smoothly parameterized reference.
- **step-local-projectors** [claim](#stmt-lem-repair-projector) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-repair-projector): Positive resolvent difference quotients and a bounded frozen-reference diagonal derivative. -> Local spectral mass bounds at both edges.
- **step-bulk-typical-edges** [claim](#stmt-lem-repair-bulk) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-repair-bulk): Dyadic slack summation, stop-path comparison and control of the frozen correction on typical vertices. -> Squared bulk-resolvent row bounds and typical tree endpoint blocks.
- **step-exact-repair** [claim](#stmt-thm-exact-repair) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-exact-repair): Bulk Schur elimination, biased pairwise-independent switch sampling, rational law approximation and finite seed enumeration. -> Vertex-disjoint valid switches yielding strict positivity at both spectral edges.
- **step-bit-complexity** [claim](#stmt-prop-bit-complexity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-bit-complexity): Rational parameter selection, determinant bit bounds, exact account comparisons and exact contrast-form inertia. -> Polynomial bit implementation of every stage and exact final spectral verification.
- **step-main-construction** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-bit-complexity-2): Assemble pairing, cleanup, boundary completion and exact repair on the same vertex set. -> The full adjacency list of a simple connected nonbipartite graph with strict Ramanujan bounds.
- **step-target-rounding** [claim](#stmt-cor-target-size) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-target-size): Apply the main theorem at the least even order above the requested target. -> A qualifying graph whose order differs from the requested target by at most the stated rounding allowance.

          "exact_quote": "At a state $x$, let $\\Omega(x)$ be a nonempty finite set of candidate transitions with a specified probability distribution. For each index $i$ in a finite set let $Z_i(x)\\ge1$ be a nonnegative account and let $D_i>0$ be its current discount. Fix nonnegative weights $a_i$. Set $$r_i=\\max\\left\\{1,\\frac{\\mathbb E[Z_i(x')\\mid x]}{Z_i(x)}\\right\\},\n \\qquad D_i'=D_i/r_i.$$ There is a candidate $x'$ such that $$\\sum_i a_iD_i'Z_i(x')\\le\\sum_i a_iD_iZ_i(x).$$ In particular, selecting a minimizer of the left side has this property. If along the selected history $\\mathbb E[Z_i(x')\\mid x]\\le(1+c_{i,t})Z_i(x)$ with $c_{i,t}\\ge0$, then $$D_{i,t}^{-1}\\le\\prod_{j<t}(1+c_{i,j})\n \\le\\exp\\left(\\sum_{j<t}c_{i,j}\\right)$$ when the initial discount is one.",

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-early-index-words",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-configuration-algebra",
        "step-path-majorants"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-early-index-words",
          "exact_quote": "Expand every occurrence of $H_0$ as $-\\sigma h F-\\sigma hJ_2/(l-2)$, every intermediate factor as $T+D_E$, and each outer factor as $P+E$. A resulting scalar word has two outer endpoint indices $s,t\\in\\{u,v\\}$, alternating swap and intermediate factors, and one of the following descriptions.*\n\n1.  *It contains a non-pure swap $-\\sigma hJ_2/(l-2)$.*\n\n2.  *All swaps are pure and it contains an off-diagonal $T$ factor, bounded by $b_{uv}$.*\n\n3.  *All swaps are pure, all $T$ factors are diagonal, and at least two intermediate factors are $D_E$.*\n\n4.  *All swaps are pure, all $T$ factors are diagonal, there is at least one intermediate factor, and there is at most one $D_E$ factor. If $s=t$, either there is a diagonal $T$ factor at $\\bar s$, or the word has exactly one intermediate factor, namely $(D_E)_{\\bar s\\bar s}$. If $s\\ne t$ and there is a $T$ factor, there is at least one local factor $j_u$ or $j_v$. If there is no $D_E$ factor, both local factors occur.*\n\n5.  *There are no intermediate factors. Its pure-swap portion has $s\\ne t$.*\n\n*Here $\\bar u=v$ and $\\bar v=u$. The list is exhaustive; the cases are made disjoint by testing them in the displayed order. With only one intermediate $D_E$ and no $T$, the exceptional same-end word in item 4 is precisely $h^2 x_i(s)x_j(s)e_{\\bar s}(\\bar s)$, where $x_i,x_j$ are the chosen outer arrays, each a $p$ or $e$ row. All estimates obtained by retaining at most a fixed number of marked factors in these cases can be summed over the remaining words with a constant independent of $n$.",
          "source_id": "PM-178.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-index-words",
      "inputs": [
        "Convergent endpoint expansions",
        "Topology and error factors"
      ],
      "method": "Classify swap and intermediate-factor index words in a disjoint testing order.",
      "output": "An exhaustive classification with a summable geometric remainder.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-early-index-words"
    },

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-early-entry-mean",

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-early-low-squares",
          "exact_quote": "For any fixed radius $R_c$, the simultaneous accounts retain $$\\begin{split}\n &T_S=\\sum_{x\\in S}e_x(x)^2\\le n^\\eta,\n \\qquad T_V=\\sum_{i\\in V}E_{ii}^2\\le n^\\eta n/l_0,\\\\\n &T_{XS,k}=\\sum_{\\substack{i\\in X,\\ x\\in S\\\\\n    \\operatorname{dist}(\\operatorname{vtx}i,\\operatorname{vtx}x)\\le k}}\n           e_i(x)^2\\le n^\\eta,\n \\qquad X\\in\\{V,S\\},\\quad 0\\le k\\le R_c .\n\\end{split}\n\\label{eq:original-23}$$ The final exponent $\\eta>0$ may be prescribed in advance and made arbitrarily small.",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-early-interpolation",
          "exact_quote": "Let a measure of bounded mass satisfy $\\int|z|^2\\le n^{-\\beta}$ and $\\int|z|^p\\le M$. For every fixed $0<t<p$, $$\\int|z|^t\\le n^{-\\beta_t}(1+M)^{t/p},\n \\qquad \\beta_t>0,\n\\label{eq:original-28}$$ for sufficiently large $n$. If a nonnegative kernel $K$ on two probability spaces has both marginal densities at most $b$, then for nonnegative $f,g$ and $r,s\\ge0$, $r+s\\le1$, $$\\iint K(x,y)f(x)^rg(y)^s\\,dx\\,dy\n \\le b\\left(\\int f\\right)^r\\left(\\int g\\right)^s.$$ For $K(i,u)=|p_i(u)|^k$, $2\\le k\\le p$, with uniform $i\\in X,u\\in S$, one may use $b=n^{\\eta_0}/l$ for any fixed $\\eta_0>0$, once $n$ is sufficiently large. The row marginal alone is at most $C_p/l$. For $k=p$ or $2p$, the constants in the row and column sums can be bounded by a constant depending only on $d$, for each fixed $p$ and large $n$.",
          "source_id": "PM-178.1"
        }

      "id": "step-interpolation",

        "Path-column and row bounds"

      "method": "Moment interpolation and Hölder bounds for kernels with controlled marginals.",

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-early-linearization",

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-early-linearization",
          "exact_quote": "For every surviving output pair, decompose its actual increment as $\\Delta E_{ij}=B_e\\mathcal L_{ij}+\\mathcal N_{ij}$, where $\\mathcal L$ is linear in $z$ and evaluated on the old output vectors. The residual contains the nonlinear terms, the formal tail, and the recentering of surviving stub outputs. It has sup $$|\\mathcal N_{ij}|\\le C(d_m^2+B_{\\rm tail}+d_m/l)$$ and conditional second moment $$\\mathbb E|\\mathcal N_{ij}|^2\n \\le C(n^\\eta/l^2+d_m^4/l).\n\\label{eq:original-29}$$ The leading terms of $\\mathcal L_{ij}$, up to their signs, are $$h p_i(u)z_{vj},\\quad h p_i(v)z_{uj},\\quad\n h p_j(u)z_{iv},\\quad h p_j(v)z_{iu},\n\\label{eq:original-30}$$ and $$h^2p_i(s)p_j(t)z_{\\bar s\\bar t},\n \\qquad s,t\\in\\{u,v\\}.\n\\label{eq:original-31}$$ All other coefficients have sup bounded by a fixed negative power of $n$. A diagonal coefficient of $H_P$ satisfies, with its first endpoint $s$ fixed, $$\\mathbb E_{\\bar s}\\bigl[|(H_P)_{ss}|^k\n                  \\mathbf 1_{\\rm valid}\\bigr]\n \\le C_p n^\\eta n^{-c'_0(k-2)}/l,\n \\qquad 2\\le k\\le p,\n\\label{eq:original-32}$$ where the expectation may also omit boundedly many output labels.",
          "source_id": "PM-178.1"
        }

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-early-phase",
          "exact_quote": "For the fixed parameters of the construction and all sufficiently large $n$, the simultaneous discounted-account rule can be run from the empty pairing down to $l_1$ unresolved stubs. It retains the structural estimates <a href=\"#eq:original-3\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:original-3\">[eq:original-3]</a>–<a href=\"#eq:original-7\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:original-7\">[eq:original-7]</a>, positivity of every tracked precision, and, at every tracked point, $$|L_1|\\le n^{.075a}/\\alpha_*,\\qquad\n |L_0|\\le n^{.105a}/\\alpha_*^2,\\qquad\n \\max_{i,j}|E_{ij}|\\le n^{.33a}/\\sqrt{l_0}.$$ The low-square estimates <a href=\"#eq:original-23\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:original-23\">[eq:original-23]</a> hold with any prescribed sufficiently small fixed loss exponent, for any fixed near radius needed in the proof. At every primary point, $$\\mathcal T \\ \\ge\\ n^{-\\theta/2}(n^{-1}+\\alpha/l)^{-1}\n\\label{eq:original-13}$$ after a fixed constant is allowed in the right-hand side.",

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-cleanup-words",

      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cleanup-words",
          "exact_quote": "After expansion of a numerical update difference and of its formal truncation tail, every term is covered by the following classes:*\n\n1.  *a term containing a non-pure swap $R$, and hence a factor $C/l$;*\n\n2.  *a pure-swap term with no intermediate endpoint factor;*\n\n3.  *a pure-swap term with exactly one endpoint $E$ factor and no topology factor;*\n\n4.  *a term with a cross topology factor $b_{uv}$;*\n\n5.  *a pure-swap term with local topology factors only, and at most one endpoint $E$ factor;*\n\n6.  *a term with at least two endpoint $E$ factors.*\n\n*In class 3 the single endpoint $E$ factor may be grouped with $W$ outers. For class 5, if both outers are at $u$, an opposite local factor $j_v$ is necessary; the symmetric statement holds for two outers at $v$. If class 5 has no endpoint $E$ factor and has distinct outers, local factors at both endpoints are necessary. All remaining factors and choices of their positions can be summed with a bounded geometric multiplier.",
          "source_id": "PM-178.1"
        }

      "output": "Exhaustive cleanup word classes with geometric summability.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-cleanup-words"

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cleanup-entry-budgets",

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-cleanup",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-early-pairing",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-cleanup",
          "exact_quote": "Assume the early-phase estimates at $l_1$, and maintain the structural estimators during cleanup. With the simultaneous discounted-account choice rule, the cleanup interval can be completed so that, at every chosen state, the precision matrices remain positive definite, <a href=\"#eq:original-8\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:original-8\">[eq:original-8]</a> holds, and $$T_S=\\sum_{x\\in S}e_x(x)^2\\leq n^\\eta,\\qquad\n |E_{ij}|\\leq2B_k.$$ The second inequality holds for every surviving pair $(i,j)$ of total level $k=\\nu(i)+\\nu(j)$. At primary points the lower estimate for $\\mathcal T$ persists in the form $$\\mathcal T\\geq n^{-\\theta}(n^{-1}+\\alpha/l)^{-1}.$$ In particular the consequences <a href=\"#eq:original-10\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:original-10\">[eq:original-10]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:original-11\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:original-11\">[eq:original-11]</a>, and a polynomial-loss form of <a href=\"#eq:original-13\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:original-13\">[eq:original-13]</a>, hold at $l_0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-178.1"
        }

    {

      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-boundary-smooth-reference",
          "exact_quote": "There are constants $r_P'>0$ and $C$ such that $$\\widehat\\Gamma_0:=V_0^T\\widehat P_0V_0\n   =I+\\widehat D_0\\quad\\hbox{on }\\mathcal H_0,\n \\qquad \\|\\widehat D_0\\|\\le \\frac{C}{R_0}.\n \\label{eq:original-40}$$ For every global vertex coordinate $i$, the vector $p_{i,0}=V_0^T\\widehat P_0e_i$ satisfies $$p_{i,0}=c_i\\Pi_{l_0}e_{s(i)}+\\tau_i,\n \\qquad |c_i|\\le1,\\qquad \\|\\tau_i\\|_2\\le n^{-r_P'}.\n \\label{eq:original-41}$$ The first summand is omitted if there is no actual path from $i$ to a boundary vertex of length less than $\\lfloor R_0/4\\rfloor$. Otherwise that vertex and that path are unique, and $c_i=(\\sigma h)^{\\operatorname{dist}(i,s(i))}$. For a boundary vertex, $s(i)$ is its own stub and $c_i=1$. The estimate for $\\tau_i$ holds also for $\\partial_h\\tau_i$, after decreasing $r_P'$ if necessary. All these statements hold uniformly throughout the closed convex hull of the retained primary parameters.",
          "source_id": "PM-178.1"
        }

      "method": "Unique short paths, boundary moment matching and tapered Chebyshev-kernel estimates.",

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-boundary-pair-averages",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-configuration-algebra"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-simple-regular-input**: The spectral certificate is applied only after checking that the adjacency matrix represents a simple regular undirected graph of the declared degree.
- **invariant-exact-contrast-certificate**: Positive definiteness of the exact quadratic form in a rational contrast basis is equivalent to the strict nonconstant spectral bound. The basis need not be orthonormal.
- **invariant-connected-nonbipartite-output**: A simple regular graph passing the strict spectral certificate is connected and nonbipartite.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-floating-point-certificate**: Accept a graph because numerical eigenvalues appear inside the desired interval. -> Approximate eigenvalues do not supply the exact strict positivity certificate used to accept a candidate.
- **anti-unchecked-graph-model**: Apply the certificate to a directed, irregular, or looped adjacency matrix as though it were a constructed graph. -> The invariant-subspace argument and graph conclusion require a simple regular graph.
- **anti-finite-tests-prove-construction**: Treat agreement on finite certificate examples as verification of the entire prescribed-order construction. -> Testing the final acceptance predicate does not establish the pairing estimates, repair hypotheses, or existence of a passing search candidate.
- **anti-local-query-guarantee**: Use the prescribed-order theorem as a local adjacency-query or jointly polynomial degree-and-size guarantee. -> Those computational guarantees are explicitly outside the stated result.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. The paper supplies a finite exact candidate acceptance test using a rational contrast basis and positive definiteness of the squared-adjacency quadratic form. This panel implements that test and its graph prerequisites; it does not implement or certify the full asymptotic construction.

```python
# INPUTS: One JSON object with integer d and square binary adjacency matrix adj.
# PRECONDITIONS: Input is finite; acceptance requires d >= 3 and a simple undirected d-regular graph.
# OUTPUTS: ACCEPT exactly when graph prerequisites and the strict contrast spectral certificate hold; otherwise REJECT.
# TIME: Polynomially many bit operations in the matrix dimension and input bit length using exact rational elimination.
# SPACE: Polynomial space for dense rational matrices and elimination intermediates.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact integers and fractions; no numerical eigenvalue approximations.
# FAILURE: Malformed input, invalid graph, or a nonpositive pivot produces REJECT.
import json
import sys
from fractions import Fraction

def certify(data):
    if not isinstance(data, dict):
        return False
    d = data.get('d')
    a = data.get('adj')
    if type(d) is not int or d < 3 or not isinstance(a, list):
        return False
    n = len(a)
    if n < 2:
        return False
    if any(not isinstance(row, list) or len(row) != n for row in a):
        return False
    if any(type(v) is not int or v not in (0, 1) for row in a for v in row):
        return False
    if any(a[i][i] != 0 for i in range(n)):
        return False
    if any(a[i][j] != a[j][i] for i in range(n) for j in range(n)):
        return False
    if any(sum(row) != d for row in a):
        return False
    q = d - 1
    ab = [[a[t][i] - a[t][n - 1] for i in range(n - 1)] for t in range(n)]
    c = [[Fraction(4 * q * (1 + int(i == j)) - sum(ab[t][i] * ab[t][j] for t in range(n))) for j in range(n - 1)] for i in range(n - 1)]
    for k in range(n - 1):
        pivot = c[k][k]
        if pivot <= 0:
            return False
        for i in range(k + 1, n - 1):
            for j in range(i, n - 1):
                value = c[i][j] - c[i][k] * c[k][j] / pivot
                c[i][j] = value
                c[j][i] = value
    return True

try:
    verdict = certify(json.load(sys.stdin))
except (ValueError, TypeError, KeyError, ArithmeticError):
    verdict = False
print('ACCEPT' if verdict else 'REJECT')
```

    "oracle_code": "import json\nimport sys\nfrom sympy import Matrix, eye\n\ndef independent_verdict(data):\n    if not isinstance(data, dict):\n        return 'REJECT'\n    degree = data.get('d')\n    rows = data.get('adj')\n    if type(degree) is not int or degree < 3 or not isinstance(rows, list):\n        return 'REJECT'\n    order = len(rows)\n    if order < 2 or any(not isinstance(row, list) or len(row) != order for row in rows):\n        return 'REJECT'\n    if any(type(entry) is not int or entry not in (0, 1) for row in rows for entry in row):\n        return 'REJECT'\n    arcs = {(u, v) for u in range(order) for v in range(order) if rows[u][v] == 1}\n    if any(u == v or (v, u) not in arcs for u, v in arcs):\n        return 'REJECT'\n    neighbors = [{v for u, v in arcs if u == vertex} for vertex in range(order)]\n    if any(len(neighborhood) != degree for neighborhood in neighbors):\n        return 'REJECT'\n    adjacency = Matrix(rows)\n    basis = Matrix(order, order - 1, lambda i, j: int(i == j) - int(i == order - 1))\n    form = basis.T * (4 * (degree - 1) * eye(order) - adjacency * adjacency) * basis\n    for size in range(1, order):\n        if form[:size, :size].det(method='berkowitz') <= 0:\n            return 'REJECT'\n    return 'ACCEPT'\n\ntry:\n    with open(sys.argv[1], encoding='utf-8') as handle:\n        computed = independent_verdict(json.load(handle))\n    with open(sys.argv[2], encoding='utf-8') as handle:\n        candidate = handle.read().strip()\n    supplied = sys.argv[3]\n    accepted = candidate == computed and supplied == computed\nexcept Exception:\n    accepted = False\nsys.exit(0 if accepted else 1)\n",

otherwise REJECT.\n# TIME: Polynomially many bit operations in the matrix dimension and input bit length using exact rational elimination.\n# SPACE: Polynomial space for dense rational matrices and elimination intermediates.\n# ARITHMETIC: Exact integers and fractions; no numerical eigenvalue approximations.\n# FAILURE: Malformed input, invalid graph, or a nonpositive pivot produces REJECT.\nimport json\nimport sys\nfrom fractions import Fraction\n\ndef certify(data):\n    if not isinstance(data, dict):\n        return False\n    d = data.get('d')\n    a = data.get('adj')\n    if type(d) is not int or d < 3 or not isinstance(a, list):\n        return False\n    n = len(a)\n    if n < 2:\n        return False\n    if any(not isinstance(row, list) or len(row) != n for row in a):\n        return False\n    if any(type(v) is not int or v not in (0, 1) for row in a for v in row):\n        return False\n    if any(a[i][i] != 0 for i in range(n)):\n        return False\n    if any(a[i][j] != a[j][i] for i in range(n) for j in range(n)):\n        return False\n    if any(sum(row) != d for row in a):\n        return False\n    q = d - 1\n    ab = [[a[t][i] - a[t][n - 1] for i in range(n - 1)] for t in range(n)]\n    c = [[Fraction(4 * q * (1 + int(i == j)) - sum(ab[t][i] * ab[t][j] for t in range(n))) for j in range(n - 1)] for i in range(n - 1)]\n    for k in range(n - 1):\n        pivot = c[k][k]\n        if pivot <= 0:\n            return False\n        for i in range(k + 1, n - 1):\n            for j in range(i, n - 1):\n                value = c[i][j] - c[i][k] * c[k][j] / pivot\n                c[i][j] = value\n                c[j][i] = value\n    return True\n\ntry:\n    verdict = certify(json.load(sys.stdin))\nexcept (ValueError, TypeError, KeyError, ArithmeticError):\n    verdict = False\nprint('ACCEPT' if verdict else 'REJECT')\n",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | An offline graph generator can implement the prescribed-order construction and return a complete adjacency list with certified strict spectral bounds. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The pairing procedure constructs a static graph. It supplies no game rules, physical evolution, or simulation fidelity contract. |
| tools | True | strong | implementable | inferred | The final positivity criterion gives an exact acceptance test for a supplied simple regular graph, independently of how that graph was generated. |
| algorithms | True | strong | implementable | inferred | Discounted accounts give a finite deterministic transition-selection construction that preserves a common weighted potential while keeping growth estimates specific to each account. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper uses adjacency lists and matrix representations but does not establish a new storage structure or operation-specific access bounds. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Polynomial bit complexity is proved for rational computations without an accelerator architecture, parallel execution contract, or hardware resource analysis. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Degree-preserving switches and spectral confinement are graph properties; the paper gives no cryptographic primitive, adversary model, or security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The local spectral mass estimate concerns adjacency eigenvectors of the constructed graph. It does not establish learned representations, training behavior, or generalization. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | A finite sequential allocation or matching problem can adapt the account minimization rule when its candidate distributions, account values, and growth budgets are explicitly computable. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The graph can serve as a candidate communication topology, but its construction supplies no distributed protocol, fault model, or consensus guarantee. |
| other (spectral graph construction and conditional spectral repair) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The exact-repair construction transforms a simple regular graph satisfying spectral confinement, local mass, and typical bulk-block hypotheses into a graph with strict bounds at both spectral edges on the same vertex set. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-fixed-degree-complexity** (formal_scope): The polynomial bit-operation guarantee fixes the degree and construction parameters. Its exponent and order threshold may depend on the degree. Blocked use: ['Claiming a running-time bound polynomial jointly in degree and vertex count.', 'Inferring a practical execution deadline from the asymptotic bit bound.']
- **limit-prescribed-even-large-order** (hypothesis): The main construction requires a sufficiently large even input order. The target-size corollary permits upward rounding rather than guaranteeing exact odd-order output. Blocked use: ['Invoking the main guarantee at an arbitrary small order.', 'Claiming exact prescribed odd-order construction from the target-size corollary.']
- **limit-full-output-interface** (formal_scope): Explicitness means producing the entire adjacency list; a local adjacency-query algorithm is outside the stated contract. Blocked use: ['Treating the construction as an on-demand neighbor-query procedure.', 'Assuming a dynamic graph-update interface.']
- **limit-account-selection-preconditions** (hypothesis): Discounted selection requires a nonempty finite candidate distribution, positive discounts, nonnegative weights and accounts bounded below. Each discount follows its own conditional growth budget. Blocked use: ['Selecting from an empty candidate set.', 'Using an undefined expectation ratio for a vanishing account.', 'Charging the worst discount loss of one account to every account.']
- **limit-excess-account-transition-contract** (hypothesis): Scalar excess control requires bounded increments and the stated drift and second-moment estimates on ordinary transitions. Exceptional surviving transitions must adjust the threshold so the account does not increase. Blocked use: ['Applying the excess estimate to unbounded increments.', 'Discarding exceptional branches without checking their threshold convention.', 'Continuing a surviving account without its required drift or variance estimate.']
- **limit-formal-series-versus-numerical-update** (formal_scope): The path generating function is a formal identity. Numerical inverse updates require their inverses to exist, and positivity preservation additionally requires a small pair-block deviation. Blocked use: ['Interpreting the formal series as an unconditional numerical convergence statement.', 'Resolving an arbitrary pair on the strength of the formal identity alone.', 'Assuming positivity of the next precision without the pair-block condition.']
- **limit-early-structural-and-bootstrap-gates** (hypothesis): The stronger path and resolvent estimates require current structural bounds, actual girth, sufficient unresolved stubs and signed positivity at the tracked parameters. Early pairing establishes these estimates inductively rather than assuming every candidate's next-state bounds. Blocked use: ['Applying structural row-square or decay estimates to an arbitrary partial pairing.', 'Using primary-only information where auxiliary parameter estimates are required.', 'Assuming candidate next-state entry bounds before the minimizing choice.']
- **limit-moment-order-dependency** (hypothesis): The horizon-growth coefficient must be fixed independently of the later high moment used for extraction. Near radii and structural losses are chosen in the specified dependency order. Blocked use: ['Choosing an extraction order from a growth coefficient that itself depends on that order.', 'Changing moment orders with graph size while retaining the fixed-parameter proof.', 'Reordering parameter choices without rechecking the strict margins.']
- **limit-cleanup-history-and-label-survival** (hypothesis): Cleanup budgets use the prescribed first endpoints, eligible initially clean partners and estimates valid through every chosen history prefix. Individual entry budgets stop when a label dies and include steps where ordinary survival has positive probability. Blocked use: ['Replacing prescribed cleanup by arbitrary partner selection without proving new budgets.', 'Using a future completion to justify current candidate statistics.', 'Continuing an alive-label budget after its label has died.', 'Counting only the ordinary branches actually selected when bounding predictable discounts.']
- **limit-separated-boundary-inputs** (hypothesis): Boundary completion requires distinct unresolved vertices without short nonempty nonbacktracking joins or returns, the retained analytic estimates and the coefficient and path-multiplicity bounds. The permission to use any final matching arises only after the remaining error block is small. Blocked use: ['Starting boundary completion from an unseparated unresolved set.', 'Matching all remaining stubs arbitrarily before the row-energy and entry-concentration phases.', 'Discarding the separation-stop path information needed for repair.']
- **limit-frozen-reference-consistency** (hypothesis): The projector estimate needs entry comparison against the same frozen reference throughout the primary range, a bounded diagonal derivative, sufficiently small comparison error and bounded geometric primary spacing. Blocked use: ['Combining unrelated reference matrices from different primary parameters.', 'Deriving local spectral mass from entry comparison without derivative control.', 'Extending the projector estimate below its stated slack regime.']
- **limit-typical-edge-additional-inputs** (hypothesis): The projector estimate supplies bulk row-square control, but the typical-edge conclusion additionally requires the separation-stop return counts, path multiplicities, reference representation and final comparison error. Blocked use: ['Deriving typical endpoint blocks from local spectral mass alone.', 'Replacing the truncated bulk reciprocal by an unrestricted inverse at the tree edge.']
- **limit-cutoff-dependent-exceptions** (formal_scope): Typical bulk endpoint blocks are asserted for each admissible cutoff pair. The exceptional edge set may depend on that pair. Blocked use: ['Assuming a common typical-edge set works simultaneously for every cutoff pair.', 'Selecting typical edges before fixing the cutoffs used in their block test.']
- **limit-repair-requires-local-inputs** (hypothesis): Exact repair applies to a simple regular graph satisfying spectral confinement, local spectral mass and typical bulk endpoint-block comparisons at both signs. A norm bound alone does not provide these inputs. Blocked use: ['Repairing an arbitrary near-Ramanujan graph using confinement alone.', 'Applying the repair guarantee to a directed, irregular or nonsimple graph.', 'Checking only one signed spectral edge.']
- **limit-valid-simultaneous-switches** (hypothesis): Repair switches require distinct endpoints and absent inserted edges. Their simultaneous use requires vertex-disjoint switches to preserve the graph contract. Blocked use: ['Applying switches with overlapping endpoint sets without a separate analysis.', 'Inserting an already present edge.', 'Allowing loops or repeated edges during repair.']
- **limit-rational-approximation-and-enumeration** (approximation): The deterministic repair search uses spectrum-separated cutoffs, controlled rational approximations and probability rounding within the proved error margins. Approximate spectral functions prepare candidates; exact positivity determines acceptance. Blocked use: ['Approximating a spectral cutoff without a separating gap.', 'Rounding the tilted law without controlling its total variation error and point probabilities.', 'Accepting a candidate solely from approximate eigenvalues.']
- **limit-exact-certificate-model** (formal_scope): The contrast-form certificate is equivalent to the strict nonconstant spectral bound only under the regular symmetric adjacency model. Strict positive definiteness is required; a zero direction does not pass. Blocked use: ["Using the certificate's graph conclusion for an irregular or directed matrix.", 'Replacing strict positive definiteness by positive semidefiniteness.', 'Treating floating-point agreement as an exact certificate.']
- **limit-finite-predicate-coverage** (formal_scope): Finite tests of the candidate acceptance predicate do not establish the pairing induction, the local repair hypotheses or the existence guarantee for every sufficiently large even order. Blocked use: ['Treating a passing finite oracle receipt as certification of the entire construction theorem.', 'Using small accepted examples to establish the unspecified large-order threshold.']
- **limit-nonbipartite-and-signing-scope** (formal_scope): The constructed graph is connected and nonbipartite. The prescribed-order pairing and switching method does not resolve the general signing problem. Blocked use: ['Using its output as a bipartite Ramanujan fixture.', 'Claiming the construction proves the Bilu–Linial signing conjecture.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For every fixed integer <i>d</i> ≥ 3, we give a deterministic algorithm that constructs a simple nonbipartite <i>d</i>-regular Ramanujan graph on every sufficiently large even number <i>n</i> of vertices. It outputs the full adjacency list in polynomially many bit operations, with an exponent that may depend on <i>d</i>. Every nonconstant adjacency eigenvalue lies strictly between $`-2\sqrt{d-1}`$ and $`2\sqrt{d-1}`$.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Nonconstant adjacency eigenvalues that are small in absolute value make the edge distribution of a regular graph close to the uniform-density prediction, and thus connect spectral estimates to expansion <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Alon1986](#ref-Alon1986)]. The Ramanujan threshold is the sharp asymptotic spectral target in fixed degree. For a simple $d$-regular graph $G$, its adjacency matrix $A_G$ has the constant eigenvector $\mathbf1$ with eigenvalue $d$. The restriction $A_G|_{\mathbf1^\perp}$ measures the remaining spectrum. Throughout this paper the desired bound is $$\bigl\|A_G|_{\mathbf1^\perp}\bigr\|\le 2\sqrt{d-1}.$$ Here $2\sqrt{d-1}$ is the spectral radius of the infinite $d$-regular tree; the Alon–Boppana bound makes it asymptotically optimal <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Alon1986](#ref-Alon1986), [Nilli1991](#ref-Nilli1991)]. Since $d>2\sqrt{d-1}$ for $d\ge3$, this bound also forces $G$ to be connected and nonbipartite.

The problem addressed here is to construct such graphs deterministically at prescribed sizes, without a positive error in the spectral bound. Our meaning of explicitness is the production of the entire adjacency list. The degree is fixed throughout the complexity analysis.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *For every integer $d\ge3$ there are constants $n_0(d)$ and $k_d$ and a deterministic algorithm with the following property. On input an even integer $n\ge n_0(d)$, the algorithm outputs the adjacency list of a simple $d$-regular graph $G$ on $n$ vertices in $O_d(n^{k_d})$ bit operations, and $$-2\sqrt{d-1}<\lambda<2\sqrt{d-1}
 \quad\text{for every eigenvalue $\lambda$ of }A_G|_{\mathbf1^\perp}.$$ In particular, $G$ is connected and nonbipartite.*

</div>

The exponent and the size threshold may depend on $d$. The statement concerns all sufficiently large even sizes; it does not assert a running-time bound polynomial jointly in $n$ and $d$, or a local adjacency-query algorithm.

<a id="stmt-cor-target-size"></a>
<div id="cor:target-size" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2**. *For every fixed $d\ge3$, a deterministic polynomial-time algorithm, on every sufficiently large integer input $N$, produces a simple connected nonbipartite $d$-regular Ramanujan graph $G$ with $N\le |V(G)|\le N+1\le2N$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-target-size"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-target-size</summary>

*Proof.* Apply Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> to the least even integer $n\ge N$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Earlier constructions and the exact threshold

Lubotzky, Phillips and Sarnak and Margulis established arithmetic constructions attaining the tree threshold in restricted degrees <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[LPS1988](#ref-LPS1988), [Margulis1988](#ref-Margulis1988)]. These constructions obtain their spectral bounds from arithmetic structure and produce infinite families on arithmetic sequences of orders. Morgenstern extended the available degrees to $q+1$ for every prime power $q$, including nonbipartite families <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Morgenstern1994](#ref-Morgenstern1994)]. These exact constructions do not cover every fixed degree and every prescribed large order simultaneously.

Bilu and Linial introduced a different approach based on graph two-lifts and signings. A two-lift replaces each vertex by two vertices and each edge by one of the two matchings between the corresponding pairs. Its new eigenvalues are those of a signed adjacency matrix, obtained by assigning a sign $+1$ or $-1$ to each edge. Their deterministic construction gives an $O(\sqrt{d\log^3 d})$ bound along iterated lifts <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[BL2006](#ref-BL2006)]. Marcus, Spielman and Srivastava then used interlacing polynomials to attain the exact bound for the largest signed eigenvalue. The bipartite Ramanujan convention excludes both $d$ and $-d$ from the spectral bound. For bipartite graphs, spectral symmetry also controls the negative edge, yielding simple bipartite Ramanujan families in every degree <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[MSS2015](#ref-MSS2015), Theorems 5.3 and 5.5]. Hall, Puder and Sawin extended the covering theorem to arbitrary lift degrees; in particular, simple bipartite $d$-regular Ramanujan graphs exist on $2dr$ vertices for every positive integer $r$ <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[HPS2018](#ref-HPS2018), Theorem 1.2 and Corollary 2.2].

The matching construction of Marcus, Spielman and Srivastava gives bipartite Ramanujan multigraphs at every even order, with strict bounds on the eigenvalues other than $\pm d$ <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[MSS2018](#ref-MSS2018), Theorem 1.1]. Cohen made this construction deterministic in polynomial time <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Cohen2016](#ref-Cohen2016)]. More recently, Cohen and Maor obtained a quantitative improvement below the tree threshold in this bipartite multigraph model <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[CM2026](#ref-CM2026), Theorem 1.2]. The restriction to $\mathbf1^\perp$ in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> includes the negative eigenvalue that bipartiteness would produce.

Recent polynomial expected-time signing algorithms of Jadbabaie, Saberi and Sra attain the sharp one-sided threshold, and hence the absolute threshold for bipartite inputs, using a Las Vegas algorithm; their general two-sided bound has an additional factor $\sqrt2$ <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[JSS2026](#ref-JSS2026), Theorem 6.1]. The Bilu–Linial conjecture asks whether every $d$-regular graph has a two-lift whose new eigenvalues all lie in $[-2\sqrt{d-1},2\sqrt{d-1}]$ <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[BL2006](#ref-BL2006), Conjecture 3.1]. Our prescribed-order construction uses partial pairings and edge switches, and does not resolve that signing problem.

For every fixed degree and every fixed $\varepsilon>0$, Mohanty, O’Donnell and Paredes gave deterministic simple constructions with bound $2\sqrt{d-1}+\varepsilon$, and order within a factor $1+o(1)$ of a requested size <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[MOP2020](#ref-MOP2020)]. Alon obtained this bound at every sufficiently large size $n$ satisfying $dn$ even <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Alon2021](#ref-Alon2021), Theorem 1.3]. His adjustment of the order deletes suitably separated vertices from a slightly larger graph and matches their neighbors; local eigenvector mass estimates control the resulting spectral error <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Alon2021](#ref-Alon2021), Section 3]. These constructions leave a positive spectral error. Removing that error requires controlling the eigenvalues on the much finer scale of their distances from both tree edges.

For every fixed degree and positive error, a uniformly random simple regular graph is near-Ramanujan with probability tending to one. This is Friedman’s theorem <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Friedman2008](#ref-Friedman2008)]; Bordenave gave a new proof using nonbacktracking operators and extended the method to random lifts <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Bordenave2020](#ref-Bordenave2020)]. Exact existence follows from the joint edge universality for uniformly random simple regular graphs in every fixed degree established by Huang, McKenzie and Yau <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[HMY2024](#ref-HMY2024), Remarks 3.10 and 3.12]. The probability of the exact two-sided Ramanujan event tends to approximately $0.69$ <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[HMY2024](#ref-HMY2024), Corollary 1.3]. Continuity of the limiting joint edge law gives the same limit for the strict interior event, so strict existence holds at every sufficiently large size with $dn$ even. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> supplies a deterministic polynomial-time construction at the exact threshold and prescribed even order.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### How the construction reaches both spectral edges

Write $q=d-1$, $s=2\sqrt q$ and $e=n^{-2/3+a}$, where $a=10^{-4}$. The first objective is a simple $d$-regular graph with nonconstant spectrum in $(-s-e/20,s+e/20)$ and enough local information to repair the remaining error. An edge switch deletes $\{u,v\}$ and $\{x,y\}$ and inserts $\{u,y\}$ and $\{x,v\}$. It is valid when the four endpoints are distinct and the inserted edges were absent. A collection of vertex-disjoint valid switches preserves simplicity, regularity and the vertex set. A norm bound alone does not determine how a switch affects an eigenvector concentrated near one of its endpoints. The construction therefore retains two kinds of local information.

To describe them, let $v_2,\ldots,v_n$ be an orthonormal eigenbasis on $\mathbf1^\perp$ for this intermediate graph, with eigenvalues $\lambda_i$, and put $x_i^\sigma=s-\sigma\lambda_i$ for $\sigma\in\{+1,-1\}$. These signed distances may be slightly negative. First, the spectral mass near either edge satisfies $$\sum_{i:x_i^\sigma\le w}|v_i(p)|^2\le Cw^{3/2}
 \qquad\text{for every vertex $p$, both signs, and $2e\le w\le2d$}.$$ Thus the modes requiring repair have little mass at any one vertex. Summing over vertices also shows that only $O(ne^{3/2})=O(n^{3a/2})$ modes lie within $O(e)$ of either edge. Thus only a small spectral subspace needs the additional positive bias. Second, choose one spectrum-avoiding bulk cutoff $w_B^\sigma$ for each sign, at a prescribed scale larger than $e$, and retain the reciprocal only beyond that cutoff: $$R_B^\sigma=\sum_{i:x_i^\sigma\ge w_B^\sigma}
                  \frac{v_iv_i^T}{x_i^\sigma}.$$ For all but a small fraction of edges $(p,p')$, the $2$-by-$2$ submatrix on their endpoints is close, entry by entry, to the tree block $$\frac1{d-2}
 \begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{d-1}&\sigma\\ \sigma&\sqrt{d-1}\end{pmatrix}.$$ The exceptional edge set may depend on the chosen pair of cutoffs. This is a local block comparison; the full inverse at the tree edge need not exist. Theorem <a href="#thm:exact-repair" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:exact-repair">34</a> states the precise scales and all three repair hypotheses.

The bulk block comparison lets us eliminate the spectral modes beyond the cutoff and express positivity as a quadratic-form condition on the remaining near-edge modes. This elimination is a Schur complement. The original near-edge form can be indefinite. At both edges, a small bias in the edge-pair distribution gives a positive mean shift to the modes closest to the threshold; the other near-edge modes retain a fixed fraction of their positive slack after the mean correction. The local mass bound controls fluctuations around this mean. The proof shows that a collection of valid simultaneous switches satisfying both strict inequalities exists, before giving a finite deterministic search for it.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### The four construction stages

Give each vertex $d$ distinguishable half-edges, called stubs. Pairing two stubs creates an edge. During the construction we maintain a positive precision matrix on $\mathbf1^\perp$ for each sign and several spectral parameters; at completion these matrices become $zI-\sigma A_G$, with $z>s$ and $\sigma=\pm1$. Their normalized inverses are compared entry by entry with truncated weighted sums of nonbacktracking paths, which do not immediately reverse an edge. Lemma <a href="#lem:discounted-accounts" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:discounted-accounts">3</a> selects each pair by minimizing a weighted sum of nonnegative statistics. Each statistic is discounted by its own computed one-step growth factor, so the same choice preserves all the required estimates.

1.  **Pair most stubs.** Starting from the empty pairing, Proposition <a href="#prop:early-phase" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:early-phase">11</a> in Section <a href="#sec:early-phase" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:early-phase">5</a> leaves a sparse set of unresolved stubs while preserving positivity, trace estimates and entrywise comparison. Its main difficulty is simultaneous control of polynomially many entries over a long sequence of choices. Lemma <a href="#lem:early-high-moments" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:early-high-moments">21</a> bounds the growth of a high moment with a coefficient independent of the moment order. Taking a sufficiently large fixed moment therefore gives the required maximum bound.

2.  **Separate the remaining stubs.** Given these estimates, Section <a href="#sec:cleanup" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:cleanup">6</a> pairs the stubs that are close to one another or to a short return. The surviving stubs occupy distinct vertices, and no short nonempty nonbacktracking path joins any two of these vertices or returns to one. The analytic estimates persist during this prescribed cleanup, by Proposition <a href="#prop:cleanup" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:cleanup">22</a> and the structural separation statement in Lemma <a href="#lemma:structure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lemma:structure">7</a>.

3.  **Complete the graph.** The separated boundary makes the compression of a smoothly truncated path reference to unresolved-stub contrasts close to the identity. These contrasts are vectors of unresolved-stub coordinates whose entries sum to zero. Each vertex row of the path reference has at most one substantial boundary coordinate. Exact low-rank inverse updates, given by Woodbury identities, continue this reference throughout completion. Section <a href="#sec:boundary-completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:boundary-completion">7</a> first reduces the squared row energies and then uses those smaller energies to improve the entry bounds. This order permits an arbitrary matching of the last few stubs. Proposition <a href="#prop:boundary-completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:boundary-completion">29</a> supplies a simple regular graph with the required spectral confinement and entry comparison against one smoothly parameterized reference. The construction retains the path information at the separation stop needed to analyze that same reference.

4.  **Remove the spectral excess.** In Section <a href="#sec:exact-repair" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:exact-repair">8</a>, the parameter dependence of the frozen reference gives the local mass bound in Lemma <a href="#lem:repair-projector" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:repair-projector">32</a>; the retained path information gives the typical endpoint blocks in Lemma <a href="#lem:repair-bulk" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:repair-bulk">33</a>. Together with confinement, these are the three inputs to Theorem <a href="#thm:exact-repair" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:exact-repair">34</a>. Its biased switches make both signed precisions strictly positive at $s$ on $\mathbf1^\perp$, while preserving simplicity, regularity and the vertex set.

Figure <a href="#fig:construction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:construction">1</a> summarizes these successive outputs.

<figure id="fig:construction">

<figcaption>The four stages on the same $n$ vertices. The first three produce the graph and a reference matrix from which the local spectral estimates for repair are extracted. Paths in the separation stage are nonempty nonbacktracking paths. All precisions act on $1^{⊥}$; positivity is tracked for both spectral signs throughout.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-construction"></a>
**Figure source and visual witness** (`fig:construction`)

Four rectangular stages arranged in a 2x2 layout connected by directional blue arrows forming a clockwise progression:
- Top-left box: "1. Early pairing" in bold, followed by "Positive signed precisions; trace and entry bounds." A horizontal blue arrow points to the right.
- Top-right box: "2. Separation" in bold, followed by "Distinct unresolved vertices; no short joins or returns." A vertical blue arrow points downward.
- Bottom-right box: "3. Boundary completion" in bold, followed by "Small row energies, then entry errors; nonconstant spectrum in (-s - e/20, s + e/20)." A horizontal blue arrow points to the left.

The deterministic selection rule belongs to the method of conditional expectations and pessimistic estimators <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Raghavan1988](#ref-Raghavan1988), [WX2008](#ref-WX2008)]. Here each statistic has its own discount, and the transition estimates control those discounts along the selected history. Weighting a path of length $j$ by $(\sigma h)^j$ introduces a scalar variable $h$. For a completed graph, the nonbacktracking path recurrence leads to the quadratic matrix $I-\sigma hA_G+(d-1)h^2I$, the regular-graph Ihara–Bass pencil <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[FZ1999](#ref-FZ1999), Sections 6 and 8]; the partial configuration instead averages over unresolved stubs, so its identities and quantitative estimates are proved directly. Likewise, degree-preserving switchings are classical in regular-graph generation <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[MW1990](#ref-MW1990)]; the local spectral inputs and the bias that improves both edges are established in the repair proof.

Local comparison with tree Green functions and stability under edge resampling were developed by Bauerschmidt, Huang and Yau for random regular graphs of sufficiently large fixed degree in the bulk <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[BHY2019](#ref-BHY2019), Sections 2, 7 and 11]. Huang and Yau extended this local-resampling approach to every fixed degree $d\ge3$ and obtained control up to the spectral edges <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[HY2024](#ref-HY2024), Sections 1.4, 3 and 5]. These are methodological precedents for the local comparisons used here; the estimates along the deterministic pairing history and the biased simultaneous repair are proved below.

Section <a href="#sec:setup" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:setup">2</a> fixes notation and parameter order; Section <a href="#sec:accounts" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:accounts">3</a> gives the selection principle; and Section <a href="#sec:foundations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:foundations">4</a> develops the configuration identities and structural estimates. Section <a href="#sec:algorithm" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:algorithm">9</a> assembles the four stages and proves polynomial bit complexity. Its final check is exact rational positivity of $B^T(4(d-1)I-A_G^2)B$, where the columns of $B$ form a rational basis of $\mathbf1^\perp$; the repair theorem ensures that the polynomial search contains a passing candidate. The structural enumeration uses color-coding <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[AYZ1995](#ref-AYZ1995)] and the perfect-hash families of Naor, Schulman and Srinivasan <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[NSS1995](#ref-NSS1995), Theorem 3(iii)]; the weighted configuration estimates and spectral repair are proved here.

<a id="sec:setup"></a>
### Conventions and parameters

Throughout the proof $d\ge3$ is fixed, $q=d-1$, $s=2\sqrt q$, and $n$ is even. Constants may depend on $d$ and on the fixed parameters and moment orders chosen below, but never on $n$. The assertion that a bound has an arbitrarily small power loss means that, for each prescribed fixed $\eta>0$, the bound holds with factor $n^\eta$ once its preceding loss parameters have been chosen sufficiently small and $n$ is sufficiently large. A bound denoted $n^{o(1)}$ has this usual meaning for every fixed positive exponent. The two uses will be kept distinct when selecting parameters.

All vertex-space precision matrices and their inverses act on $\mathbf1^\perp$. For coordinate entries and traces we extend such an operator by zero on the constant vector. Stub-space operators on a contrast subspace are extended by zero in the same way. The distinction between the identity on a contrast subspace and its zero extension is understood in all compressed identities.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Matrices used during the construction

The matrix estimates use the inverse of a precision matrix assigned to the current partial pairing; its normalized form will be denoted by $W$. The first reference, denoted by $P$, is a finite weighted sum of nonbacktracking paths, with degrees below the cutoff $L$ fixed below. Through cleanup the entry error is $E=W-P$. At the separated boundary we replace this path sum by a smooth truncation $\widehat P_0$ and continue it by exact inverse updates, obtaining $\widehat P$; the error is then $E=W-\widehat P$. The precise matrix definitions and update identities begin in Section <a href="#sec:foundations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:foundations">4</a>. No positivity of $\widehat P$ is needed.

The symbols below are defined in full where their objects first enter the proof; the table serves as a reference when moving between stages.

<div class="center">

| Notation             | Meaning and definition                                                                                                                                                                              |
|:---------------------|:----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| $S,l$                | Current unresolved stubs and their number; Section <a href="#sec:foundations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:foundations">4</a>.                                                     |
| $\Xi,\mathcal D$     | Centered incidence and $\mathcal D=\Xi\Xi^T$; Section <a href="#sec:foundations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:foundations">4</a>.                                                  |
| $M,W$                | Signed precision and its normalized inverse; Section <a href="#sec:foundations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:foundations">4</a>.                                                   |
| $P,E$                | Truncated path sum and $E=W-P$; Lemma <a href="#lemma:configuration-algebra" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lemma:configuration-algebra">6</a>.                                          |
| $\widehat P,E$       | Continued smooth reference and $E=W-\widehat P$; Section <a href="#sec:boundary-completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:boundary-completion">7</a>.                               |
| $\Gamma,D$           | Stub compression $\Gamma=\Xi^TW\Xi$ and $D=\Gamma-\Pi_l$; $D$ is distinct from $\mathcal D$.                                                                                                        |
| $L_0,L_1,\mathcal T$ | Centered traces and $\mathcal T=\operatorname{Tr}(\Xi^TW^2\Xi)$; Section <a href="#subsec:resolvent-consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="subsec:resolvent-consequences">4.5</a>. |

</div>

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Stopping sizes and spectral parameters

The unresolved-stub counts decrease as $dn\to l_1\to l_0\to l_2\to l_3\to0$. The first two stops delimit early pairing and cleanup; $l_2$ separates the two boundary phases, and $l_3$ is the final arbitrary matching. Fix $a=10^{-4}$ and put $e=n^{-2/3+a}$. The following table records the principal scales. The relation $\asymp$ permits fixed positive factors and integer rounding.

<div class="center">

| Quantity   | Scale                        | Use                         |
|:-----------|:-----------------------------|:----------------------------|
| $l_0$      | $n^{2/3+.15a}$               | Separated boundary size     |
| $b$        | $\lceil n^{1/3+.345a}\rceil$ | Cleanup steps               |
| $l_1$      | $l_0+2b$                     | End of early pairing        |
| $q^{L/2}$  | $\sqrt{l_0}\,n^{-.29a}$      | Path cutoff                 |
| $q^{R_0}$  | $n^{.04a}$                   | Boundary separation         |
| $q^{g_0}$  | $n^{.002a}$                  | Girth exclusion             |
| $\alpha_*$ | $n^{-1/3+a/2}$               | Closest precision parameter |
| $l_2$      | $l_0 n^{-g-r}$               | End of boundary phase A     |
| $l_3$      | $n^\chi$                     | End of concentrated updates |

</div>

The quantities $l_0,l_1,l_2,l_3$ are even. A logarithmic radius or cutoff is rounded to an integer, changing its associated power of $q$ by a fixed factor. We keep path degrees $<L$.

For $0<h<1/\sqrt q$ define $$z(h)=h^{-1}+qh,\qquad \alpha(h)=1-qh^2,
 \qquad m_h=\frac{h}{1-h^2}.$$ Choose the largest primary parameter $h_*$ with $\alpha(h_*)\asymp n^{-1/3+a/2}$ and $0<z(h_*)-s<e/20$. Write $\alpha_* = \alpha(h_*)$. Successive smaller primary parameters have $1-\sqrt qh$ increasing by a fixed factor, for instance a factor of two, up to a sufficiently small fixed constant. Consequently there are $O(\log n)$ primaries, their $z-s$ scales cover $[e/20,c]$ up to fixed factors, and successive positive scales have ratios bounded above and bounded away from one. Every parameter remains in a fixed compact subinterval of $(0,1)$.

During the early and cleanup phases, add an auxiliary point immediately below each primary, at distance $$b'=n^{-.16a}\alpha(h).$$ At the largest primary also use $h_*-j b_h$, $1\le j\le k_h$, where $$b_h=n^{-.03a}\alpha_*,\qquad
 k_h=\left\lceil\frac{10}{.03a}\right\rceil.$$ The $j$-spacing is exact. These auxiliary groups are disjoint for large $n$. Both signs $\sigma\in\{1,-1\}$ are used at every tracked parameter. Only the primary points are retained after $l_0$ unresolved stubs remain.

<a id="subsec:hierarchy"></a>
#### Order of the constant choices

The stopping sizes and spectral parameters above specify the stages of the construction. The remaining constants are introduced with the estimates they control in Sections <a href="#sec:early-phase" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:early-phase">5</a>–<a href="#sec:boundary-completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:boundary-completion">7</a>. The following list records the dependencies among those choices, for reference when checking that every constant is fixed independently of $n$. In particular, a coefficient governing growth over a whole phase must be fixed before the higher moment order used to extract an individual bound. The list is a dependency guide; the indicated later arguments define the comparators, row bounds, and exceptional events.

1.  Boundary phase A (Section <a href="#subsec:boundary-phase-a" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="subsec:boundary-phase-a">7.4</a>) uses the fixed $d,a$ and spectral moment order $k=8$. Its initial row bounds have constants depending only on $d$; the row comparison constants, including the finite enlargements at exceptional events, are fixed at this stage.

2.  The smooth reference of Section <a href="#subsec:boundary-reference" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="subsec:boundary-reference">7.1</a> has a row-tail exponent $r_P>0$; it is permissible to take $r_P=.001a$. The exponents $g=r>0$ are chosen sufficiently small relative to $a,r_P$ and the finitely many fixed drift constants in phase A. These constants depend on $k,d$ and the fixed row bounds, not on the higher scalar moment orders.

3.  In phase A the comparator exponent $\zeta_X$ is chosen as in <a href="#eq:original-50" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:original-50">[eq:original-50]</a>, followed by $\theta>0$ sufficiently small compared with $g$. The row-barrier exponents satisfy $0<r_v<r_s<r_g<r/2$ with strict gaps. The final matching exponent $\chi$ satisfies $g+r\ll\chi<r_P$ and is larger than these row-barrier exponents.

4.  In Section <a href="#sec:cleanup" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:cleanup">6</a>, the exponent $\rho>0$ is small enough that $20\rho$ is below the separation and scale margins. For each scalar concentration account, its normalization exponent is smaller than its drift and variance margins; its fixed even moment order is then chosen large enough for extraction over its polynomial-size family.

5.  For the early high-moment account of Lemma <a href="#lem:early-high-moments" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:early-high-moments">21</a>, a sufficiently large fixed even $p$ is chosen using the uniform growth coefficient in <a href="#eq:original-33" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:original-33">[eq:original-33]</a>. Its diagonal weight and outer-entry threshold are fixed next, determining the finite near radius $R_c$. The constants used to choose this threshold depend only on $p,d$, the finite splitting inequalities, and the uniform row bounds. Constants depending on the resulting $R_c$ occur only in terms already accompanied by a negative power of $n$.

6.  Finally, the structural and low-square loss exponents are chosen small enough for these fixed moment orders and for the finite induction over near radii. The fixed diagram excess cutoff is large enough for all the moments that have been used.

There is no dependence of a polynomial horizon-growth coefficient on a later scalar extraction order. The estimates below record this fact at the points where it is essential. All strict comparisons are unchanged if scales with rational exponents are replaced by dyadic rational approximations within fixed factors. Rational choices of the parameters $h$ with $O_d(\log n)$ bits, including exact equally spaced auxiliaries, will be used for the algorithm.

<a id="sec:accounts"></a>
### Simultaneous deterministic choice

A single pairing must preserve several estimates at once. We select it by minimizing a weighted sum of nonnegative statistics, called accounts. Each account receives its own discount, computed from its exact conditional expectation. This separates the common choice from the different growth estimates used to analyze its consequences. The underlying method of conditional expectations and pessimistic estimators is classical <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[Raghavan1988](#ref-Raghavan1988)]; see also <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[WX2008](#ref-WX2008), Section 3]. The lemma below includes the account-specific discount update in the finite averaging argument.

<a id="stmt-lem-discounted-accounts"></a>
<div id="lem:discounted-accounts" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3** (Discounted accounts). *At a state $x$, let $\Omega(x)$ be a nonempty finite set of candidate transitions with a specified probability distribution. For each index $i$ in a finite set let $Z_i(x)\ge1$ be a nonnegative account and let $D_i>0$ be its current discount. Fix nonnegative weights $a_i$. Set $$r_i=\max\left\{1,\frac{\mathbb E[Z_i(x')\mid x]}{Z_i(x)}\right\},
 \qquad D_i'=D_i/r_i.$$ There is a candidate $x'$ such that $$\sum_i a_iD_i'Z_i(x')\le\sum_i a_iD_iZ_i(x).$$ In particular, selecting a minimizer of the left side has this property. If along the selected history $\mathbb E[Z_i(x')\mid x]\le(1+c_{i,t})Z_i(x)$ with $c_{i,t}\ge0$, then $$D_{i,t}^{-1}\le\prod_{j<t}(1+c_{i,j})
